6.2.2
Cheap HSIC vs. asymptotic cross-HSIC testing

A recently-proposed alternative to the HSIC wild bootstrap test is the
asymptotic cross-HSIC test

[
57
]
which avoids wild bootstrapping by thresholding a related, asymptotically-normal test statistic using a Gaussian quantile.
By avoiding wild bootstrapping, the asymptotic cross-HSIC test achieves a substantial speed-up over a standard HSIC wild bootstrap test. However, as
Shekhar, Kim and Ramdas [57]
note, this comes at the cost of reduced power and the loss of finite-sample validity.
In
Fig.

7
, we see that cheap HSIC achieves speed-up benefits comparable to those of asymptotic cross-HSIC (right) while also preserving the higher power and final-sample validity of standard HSIC testing for any given sample size (left).

7
Discussion and extensions

We have presented a general framework,
cheap permutation testing
, for accelerating hypothesis testing while preserving the high power and exact false positive control of standard permutation tests. The core idea is to group datapoints into bins and to exploit bin-wise sufficient statistics to avoid the standard sample-size-dependent overhead of permutation testing.
A notable limitation of this approach is that compact sufficient are not available for all unstructured or black-box test statistics.
However, they are available for the quadratic test statistics popular in two-sample homogeneity and independence testing and for a variety of more general structured classes:

Generalized quadratic test statistics

Many popular test statistics can be recovered as simple transformations
of quadratic statistics. For example,
studentized
U and V-statistics, which are known to imbue permutation tests with additional robustness properties
[
45
,
30
,
10
,
11
]
, divide a standard U or V-statistic by an estimate of its standard error and commonly take the form
U/\sqrt{V}
where both
U
and
V
are quadratic test statistics in the sense of
Def.

1
.
All functions of one or more quadratic test statistics are amenable to the binned sufficient statistic constructions of
Sec.

3
and hence can benefit from the speed-ups of cheap testing.

Multisample /
K
-sample testing

In multisample homogeneity testing one observes
K\geq 2
independent samples drawn i.i.d. from unknown distribution
\mathbb{P}_{1},\dots,\mathbb{P}_{K}
and seeks to test whether all
K
distributions match.
Similarly, in multisample independence testing one observes tuples of
K\geq 2
variables and seeks to test whether the components are jointly independent.
In each case, one can generalize the cheap permutation tests of
Algs.

1
and
2
to accommodate the multisample U and V-statistics commonly employed
[
47
,
46
,
10
, see, e.g.,]
.

Higher-order test statistics

Cheap testing is also compatible with
polynomial test statistics
, that is, degree
p
polynomial functionals of the sample empirical distributions
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{K}
:

\textstyle T=\sum_{j_{1},\dots,j_{p}\in[K]}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{j_{1}}\times\dots\times\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{j_{p}})\phi_{j_{1},\dots,j_{p}}.

(74)

In this case,
O(s^{p})
sufficient statistics would suffice to run a cheap test with
O(Bs^{p})
permutation overhead, a substantial improvement over the standard
\Theta(Bn^{p})
permutation overhead.

{supplement}
\stitle

Supplement to “Cheap Permutation Testing”

\sdescription
This supplement contains proofs of all results as well as supplementary experiment details.

Appendix A
Additional notation

For any dataset
\mathbb{X}={(X_{i})}_{i=1}^{n}
, we let
\delta_{\mathbb{X}}
denote the empirical distribution
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{X_{i}}
and

\mathbb{X}_{-k}=(X_{1},\dots,X_{k-1},X_{k+1},\dots,X_{n})
for each
k\in[n]
.
For a given positive-definite kernel
\mathbf{k}
, we let
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{k}}
represent the associated reproducing kernel Hilbert space
[RKHS,
59
, Def. 4.18]
and
\|{\cdot}\|_{\mathbf{k}}
and
\langle{\cdot},{\cdot}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}
represent the RKHS norm and inner product
[
59
, Thm. 4.21]
.

A.1
Additional notation for cheap homogeneity testing (
Alg.

1
)

Let
\mathbb{X}
be the concatenation of
\mathbb{Y}
and
\mathbb{Z}
. We define the number of elements per bin
m\triangleq\frac{n}{s}=\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{s}
, the number of bins for each sample
s_{1}\triangleq\frac{n_{1}}{m}
and
s_{2}\triangleq\frac{n_{2}}{m}
, and the datapoint bins
\mathbb{Y}^{(i)}=(Y_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
and
\mathbb{Z}^{(i)}=(Z_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
.

For for the homogeneity U-statistic of
Def.

2
, we define the bin kernel

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{Y}^{(i)},\mathbb{Y}^{(j)};\mathbb{Z}^{(i)},\mathbb{Z}^{(j)})=\frac{1}{m^{4}}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Y}^{(i)},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Y}^{(j)},z\in\mathbb{Z}^{(i)},z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{(j)}}h_{\mathrm{ho}}(y,y^{\prime};z,z^{\prime}).\end{split}

Moreover, by the definition of
h_{\mathrm{ho}}
, we can rewrite
H_{\mathrm{ho},m}
as

\textstyle H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{Y}^{(i)},\mathbb{Y}^{(j)};\mathbb{Z}^{(i)},\mathbb{Z}^{(j)})

\textstyle=G_{m}(\mathbb{Y}^{(i)},\mathbb{Y}^{(j)})+G_{m}(\mathbb{Z}^{(i)},\mathbb{Z}^{(j)})-G_{m}(\mathbb{Y}^{(i)},\mathbb{Z}^{(j)})-G_{m}(\mathbb{Y}^{(j)},\mathbb{Z}^{(i)}),

(75)

where

\textstyle G_{m}(\mathbb{Y}^{(i)},\mathbb{Y}^{(j)})\triangleq\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Y}^{(i)},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Y}^{(j)}}g(y,y^{\prime}).

(76)

Observe that
H_{\mathrm{ho},m}
can also be written in terms of
\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}
:

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{Y}^{(i)},\mathbb{Y}^{(j)};\mathbb{Z}^{(i)},\mathbb{Z}^{(j)})=\frac{1}{m^{4}}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Y}^{(i)},y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Y}^{(j)},z\in\mathbb{Z}^{(i)},z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{(j)}}\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(y,y^{\prime};z,z^{\prime}).\end{split}

Let
(\mathbb{X}^{(i)})_{i=1}^{s}
denote the concatenation of
(\mathbb{Y}^{(i)})_{i=1}^{s_{1}}
and
(\mathbb{Z}^{(i)})_{i=1}^{s_{2}}
.
For an independent, uniformly random permutation
\pi
of
[s]
, we also define the
cheaply-permuted two-sample V and U-statistics
as

\textstyle V_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{s_{1}^{2}s_{2}^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{s_{2}}H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{X}^{(\pi_{i_{1}})},\mathbb{X}^{(\pi_{i_{2}})};\mathbb{X}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{1}})},\mathbb{X}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{2}})})\quad\text{and}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

(77)

\textstyle U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{(s_{1})_{(2)}(s_{2})_{(2)}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{s_{1}}}\sum_{(j_{1},j_{2})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{s_{2}}}H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{X}^{(\pi_{i_{1}})},\mathbb{X}^{(\pi_{i_{2}})};\mathbb{X}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{1}})},\mathbb{X}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{2}})}).\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

(78)

These expressions along with the substitutions
n\leftarrow s
,
n_{1}\leftarrow s_{1}
,
n_{2}\leftarrow s_{2}
,
h_{\mathrm{ho}}\leftarrow H_{\mathrm{ho},m}
, and
g\leftarrow G_{m}
help us to frame a cheap homogeneity U-statistic permutation test as a standard homogeneity U-statistic permutation test operating on bins of datapoints.

A.2
Additional notation for cheap independence testing (
Alg.

2
)

We define the number of elements per bin
m\triangleq\frac{n}{s}
and the datapoint bins
\mathbb{Y}^{(i)}=(Y_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
,
\mathbb{Z}^{(i)}=(Z_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
, and

\mathbb{X}^{(i)}=((Y_{j},Z_{j}))_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
.
When convenient, we will also refer to the
m
elements of these bins as
(Y^{(i)}_{j})_{j=1}^{m}
,
(Z^{(i)}_{j})_{j=1}^{m}
,
and

(X^{(i)}_{j})_{j=1}^{m}
.

For the independence V-statistic of
Def.

3
, we
define the bin kernel
H_{\mathrm{in},m}
as

\textstyle H_{\mathrm{in},m}(\mathbb{X}^{(1)},\mathbb{X}^{(2)},\mathbb{X}^{(3)},\mathbb{X}^{(4)})=\frac{1}{m^{4}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}=1}^{m}h_{\mathrm{in}}(X^{(1)}_{i_{1}},X^{(2)}_{i_{2}},X^{(3)}_{i_{3}},X^{(4)}_{i_{4}})

(79)

where, for

\textstyle g((y,z),(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}))\triangleq g_{Y}(y,y^{\prime})g_{Z}(x,z^{\prime}),

(80)

we can write

\textstyle h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4})=

\textstyle\iint g((y,z),(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}))\,d(\delta_{(Y_{1},Z_{1})}+\delta_{(Y_{4},Z_{4})}-\delta_{(Y_{1},Z_{4})}-\delta_{(Y_{4},Z_{1})})(y,z)

(81)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad d(\delta_{(Y_{2},Z_{2})}+\delta_{(Y_{3},Z_{3})}-\delta_{(Y_{2},Z_{3})}-\delta_{(Y_{3},Z_{2})})(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}).

(82)

An alternative way to express
h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4})
is the following:

\textstyle h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4})

\textstyle=h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{4})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3},Z_{4}),\quad\text{for}\quad

(83)

\textstyle h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{4})

\textstyle\triangleq g_{Y}(y_{1},y_{2})+g_{Y}(y_{3},y_{4})-g_{Y}(y_{1},y_{3})-g_{Y}(y_{2},y_{4}),

(84)

\textstyle h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3},Z_{4})

\textstyle\triangleq g_{Z}(z_{1},z_{2})+g_{Z}(z_{3},z_{4})-g_{Z}(z_{1},z_{3})-g_{Z}(z_{2},z_{4}).

(85)

Using this, we can rewrite
H_{\mathrm{in},m}
as

\textstyle H_{\mathrm{in},m}(\mathbb{X}^{(1)},\mathbb{X}^{(2)},\mathbb{X}^{(3)},\mathbb{X}^{(4)})

(86)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{m^{4}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}=1}^{m}\iint g((y,z),(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}))\,d(\delta_{(Y^{(1)}_{i_{1}},Z^{(1)}_{i_{1}})}+\delta_{(Y^{(4)}_{i_{4}},Z^{(4)}_{i_{4}})}-\delta_{Y^{(1)}_{i_{1}},Z^{(4)}_{i_{4}}}-\delta_{Y^{(4)}_{i_{4}},Z^{(1)}_{i_{1}}})(y,z)

(87)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad d(\delta_{(Y^{(2)}_{i_{2}},Z^{(2)}_{i_{2}})}+\delta_{(Y^{(3)}_{i_{3}},Z^{(3)}_{i_{3}})}-\delta_{(Y^{(2)}_{i_{2}},Z^{(3)}_{i_{3}})}-\delta_{(Y^{(3)}_{i_{3}},Z^{(2)}_{i_{2}})})(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})

(88)

\textstyle=\iint g((y,z),(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}))\,d(\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{4}=1}^{m}\delta_{(Y^{(1)}_{i_{1}},Z^{(1)}_{i_{1}})}+\delta_{(Y^{(4)}_{i_{4}},Z^{(4)}_{i_{4}})}-\delta_{Y^{(1)}_{i_{1}},Z^{(4)}_{i_{4}}}-\delta_{Y^{(4)}_{i_{4}},Z^{(1)}_{i_{1}}})(y,z)

(89)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad d(\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{i_{2},i_{3}=1}^{m}\delta_{(Y^{(2)}_{i_{2}},Z^{(2)}_{i_{2}})}+\delta_{(Y^{(3)}_{i_{3}},Z^{(3)}_{i_{3}})}-\delta_{(Y^{(2)}_{i_{2}},Z^{(3)}_{i_{3}})}-\delta_{(Y^{(3)}_{i_{3}},Z^{(2)}_{i_{2}})})(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})

(90)

\textstyle=\iint g((y,z),(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}))\,d(\delta_{(\mathbb{Y}^{(1)},\mathbb{Z}^{(1)})}+\delta_{(\mathbb{Y}^{(4)},\mathbb{Z}^{(4)})}-\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(1)}}\times\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(4)}}-\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(4)}}\times\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(1)}})(y,z)

(91)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad d(\delta_{(\mathbb{Y}^{(2)},\mathbb{Z}^{(2)})}+\delta_{(\mathbb{Y}^{(3)},\mathbb{Z}^{(3)})}-\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(2)}}\times\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(3)}}-\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(3)}}\times\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(2)}})(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})

(92)

For a permutation
\pi
of
[s]
, we can also define the
cheaply-permuted independence V-statistic
,

\textstyle V_{n}^{\pi,s}\triangleq\frac{1}{s^{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in[s]^{4}}H_{\mathrm{in},m}\big(

\textstyle(\mathbb{Y}^{(i_{1})},\mathbb{Z}^{(\pi_{i_{1}})}),(\mathbb{Y}^{(i_{2})},\mathbb{Z}^{(\pi_{i_{2}})}),

(93)

\textstyle(\mathbb{Y}^{(i_{3})},\mathbb{Z}^{(\pi_{i_{3}})}),(\mathbb{Y}^{(i_{4})},\mathbb{Z}^{(\pi_{i_{4}})})\big).

(94)

These expressions along with the substitutions
s
for
n
and
H_{\mathrm{in},m}
for
h_{\mathrm{in}}
help us to frame a cheap independence V-statistic permutation test as a standard independence V-statistic permutation test operating on bins of datapoints.

Appendix B
Proof of

Thm.

1
:
Theorem 1

(Power of cheap homogeneity: finite variance)
.

We will establish both claims using the refined two moments method (
Corollary

1
) with the auxiliary sequence
(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi_{b},s})_{b=1}^{B}
of cheaply-permuted two-sample U-statistics
78
.
The expression
76
for
H_{\mathrm{ho},m}
ensures that

\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}\mid\mathbb{X}]=0
surely for all
s\geq 4
, so, by
Corollary

1
, it suffices to bound
\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}})
and
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}\mid\mathbb{X})]
.

Lem.

1
, proved in
Sec.

B.1
, gives the unconditional variance bound.

Lemma 1

(Variance of homogeneity U-statistics)
.

Under the assumptions of
Thm.

1
,

\textstyle\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}})\leq\frac{4\psi_{Y,1}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\psi_{Z,1}}{n_{2}}+\frac{(17n_{1}n_{2}+4n_{2}^{2}+4n_{1}^{2})\psi_{YZ,2}}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}.

(95)

Lem.

2
, proved in
Sec.

B.2
, provides the conditional variance bounds.

Lemma 2

(Conditional variance of permuted homogeneity U-statistics)
.

Under the assumptions of
Thm.

1
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,n}

\textstyle|\mathbb{X})]\leq\frac{2n_{1}^{2}+2n_{2}^{2}+16n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}\quad\text{and}\quad

(96)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X})]

\textstyle\leq\big(\frac{2n_{2}^{2}+2n_{1}^{2}+16n_{1}n_{2}+400(n^{2}_{1}+n^{2}_{2})/s}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\!+\!\frac{352n^{4}/s}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}}\big)\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}

(97)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{8n^{3}m^{3}\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}+96n^{3}m^{2}\max\{\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\}}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}}.

(98)

Corollaries

1
,
1
, and
2
together imply power at least
1-\beta
for standard testing if

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]&\textstyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{3-\beta}{\beta}\big(\frac{4\psi_{Y,1}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\psi_{Z,1}}{n_{2}}+\frac{(17n_{1}n_{2}+4n_{2}^{2}+4n_{1}^{2})\psi_{YZ,2}}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\big)}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha^{\star}}{\alpha^{\star}}\frac{(6n_{1}^{2}+6n_{2}^{2}+48n_{1}n_{2})\psi_{YZ,2}}{\beta n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}},\end{split}

a condition implied by our assumption
35
, and power at least
1-\beta
for cheap testing if

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]&\textstyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{3-\beta}{\beta}\big(\frac{4\psi_{Y,1}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\psi_{Z,1}}{n_{2}}+\frac{(17n_{1}n_{2}+4n_{2}^{2}+4n_{1}^{2})\psi_{YZ,2}}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\big)}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle+\big(\frac{(\alpha^{\star})^{-1}-1}{\beta}\big(\frac{6n_{2}^{2}+6n_{1}^{2}+48n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\!+\!\frac{1200(n_{1}-1)(n_{2}-1)(n^{2}_{1}+n^{2}_{2})/s+1056n^{3}m}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}}\big)\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle+\frac{288((\alpha^{\star})^{-1}-1)n^{3}m^{2}\max\{\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\}}{\beta n_{1}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}}\big)^{1/2}+\sqrt{\frac{24((\alpha^{\star})^{-1}-1)n^{3}m^{3}\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}}{\beta n_{1}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}}}.\end{split}

We can rewrite
App.

B
equivalently as

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]&\textstyle\geq\big(1-\sqrt{\frac{24((\alpha^{\star})^{-1}-1)n^{3}m^{3}}{\beta n_{1}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}}}\big)^{-1}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{3-\beta}{\beta}\big(\frac{4\psi_{Y,1}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\psi_{Z,1}}{n_{2}}+\frac{(17n_{1}n_{2}+4n_{2}^{2}+4n_{1}^{2})\psi_{YZ,2}}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\big)}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\ +\big(\frac{(\alpha^{\star})^{-1}-1}{\beta}\big(\frac{6n_{2}^{2}+6n_{1}^{2}+48n_{1}n_{2}+1200(n^{2}_{1}+n^{2}_{2})/s}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\!+\!\frac{1056n^{3}m}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}}\big)\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad+\frac{288((\alpha^{\star})^{-1}-1)n^{3}m^{2}\max\{\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\}}{\beta n_{1}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}}\big)^{1/2}\bigg).\end{split}

To conclude, we note that our assumption
34
is a sufficient condition for
App.

B
as

\textstyle n^{4}

\textstyle=(n_{1}^{2}+n_{2}^{2})^{2}+4n_{1}n_{2}(n_{1}^{2}+n_{2}^{2})+4n_{1}^{2}n_{2}^{2}\geq 8n_{1}n_{2}(n_{1}^{2}+n_{2}^{2})\geq 8(n_{1}-1)(n_{2}-1)(n_{1}^{2}+n_{2}^{2}).

(99)

B.1
Proof of

Lem.

1
:
Lemma 1

(Variance of homogeneity U-statistics)
.

By
[
38
, p. 38]
,

\textstyle\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{1}})

\textstyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{2}{2\choose i}{2\choose j}{n_{1}-2\choose 2-i}{n_{2}-2\choose 2-j}{n_{1}\choose 2}^{-1}{n_{2}\choose 2}^{-1}\hat{\sigma}_{i,j}^{2}

(100)

\textstyle=16\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\frac{(n_{1}-2)!^{2}}{n_{1}!i!(2-i)!^{2}(n_{1}-4+i)!}\frac{(n_{2}-2)!^{2}}{n_{2}!j!(2-j)!^{2}(n_{2}-4+j)!}\hat{\sigma}_{i,j}^{2}\quad\text{where}\quad

(101)

\textstyle\hat{\sigma}_{i,j}^{2}

\textstyle\triangleq\mathrm{Var}(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{1},Y_{2};Z_{1},Z_{2})|Y_{i+1},\dots,Y_{2},Z_{j+1},\dots,Z_{2}])\quad\text{for}\quad i,j\in[2].

(102)

Note that for
n_{1}\geq 4
(and analogously for
n_{2}\geq 4
),

\textstyle\frac{(n_{1}-2)!^{2}}{n_{1}!i!(2-i)!^{2}(n_{1}-4+i)!}=\begin{cases}\frac{(n_{1}-2)!^{2}}{4n_{1}!(n_{1}-4)!}=\frac{(n_{1}-2)(n_{1}-3)}{4n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}\leq\frac{1}{4}&\text{if }i=0\\
\frac{(n_{1}-2)!^{2}}{n_{1}!(n_{1}-3)!}=\frac{n_{1}-2}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}\leq\frac{1}{n_{1}}&\text{if }i=1\\
\frac{(n_{1}-2)!^{2}}{2n_{1}!(n_{1}-2)!}=\frac{1}{2n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}&\text{if }i=2\end{cases}.

(103)

Plugging this into the right-hand side of
101
, we obtain that for
n_{1},n_{2}\geq 4
,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}})&\textstyle\leq 16\big(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{0,0}^{2}}{4^{2}}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{0,1}^{2}}{4n_{2}}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{1,0}^{2}}{4n_{1}}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{1,1}^{2}}{n_{1}n_{2}}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{0,2}^{2}}{8n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,0}^{2}}{8n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{1,2}^{2}}{2n_{1}n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,1}^{2}}{2n_{2}n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}+\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,2}^{2}}{4n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\frac{4\hat{\sigma}_{1,0}^{2}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\hat{\sigma}_{0,1}^{2}}{n_{2}}+\big(16+\frac{4}{9}\big)\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,2}^{2}}{n_{1}n_{2}}+\big(2+2\big)\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,2}^{2}}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}+\big(2+2\big)\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{2,2}^{2}}{n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\frac{4\hat{\sigma}_{1,0}^{2}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\hat{\sigma}_{0,1}^{2}}{n_{2}}+\frac{(17n_{1}n_{2}+4n_{2}^{2}+4n_{1}^{2})\hat{\sigma}_{2,2}^{2}}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}.\end{split}

In the second inequality, we used that
\hat{\sigma}_{0,0}^{2}=0
and that, by the law of total variance,
\hat{\sigma}_{i,j}^{2}\leq\hat{\sigma}_{2,2}^{2}
for all
i,j\in[2]
. Equation
95
then follows from the fact that
\hat{\sigma}_{1,0}^{2}\leq\psi_{Y,1}=
,
\hat{\sigma}_{0,1}^{2}\leq\psi_{Z,1}
, and
\hat{\sigma}_{2,2}^{2}\leq\psi_{YZ,2}
by their definitions.

B.2
Proof of

Lem.

2
:
Lemma 2

(Conditional variance of permuted homogeneity U-statistics)
.

We begin by defining the convenient shorthand

\textstyle\mathfrak{i}\triangleq(i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2},i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})\triangleq|\{i_{1},i_{2}\}\cap\{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime}\}|+|\{j_{1},j_{2}\}\cap\{j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}\}|.

(104)

B.2.1
Proof of
96

Following the proof of
[
31
, Thm. 4.1]
, we define the index sets

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}&\textstyle\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{8}_{+}:(i_{1},i_{2})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{n_{1}},(j_{1},j_{2})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{n_{2}},(i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{n_{1}},(j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{n_{2}}\},\\
\textstyle\mathsf{I}_{1}&\textstyle\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})\leq 1\},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{I}_{1}^{c}=\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})>1\}.\end{split}

By the argument of
[
31
, Thm. 4.1]
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,n}|\mathbb{X})]\leq\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}\frac{|\mathsf{I}_{1}^{c}|}{(n_{1})^{2}_{(2)}(n_{2})^{2}_{(2)}},

(105)

where
\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}
is the maximum over
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{1}^{c}
of

\textstyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\pi_{i_{1}}},X_{\pi_{i_{2}}};X_{\pi_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\pi_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\pi_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\pi_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\pi_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\pi_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]\big|,

(106)

and
\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}
is the symmetrized version of
h_{\mathrm{ho}}
defined in
30
.
Moreover,

\textstyle|\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}|

\textstyle=n_{1}^{2}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}^{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2},

(107)

\textstyle|\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})=0\}|

\textstyle=n_{1}(n_{1}-1)(n_{1}-2)(n_{1}-3)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)(n_{2}-2)(n_{2}-3),\quad\text{and}\quad

(108)

\textstyle|\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})=1\}|

\textstyle=4n_{1}(n_{1}-1)(n_{1}-2)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)((n_{2}-2)(n_{2}-3)+(n_{1}-2)(n_{1}-3)),

(109)

and therefore

\textstyle|\mathsf{I}_{1}^{c}|

\textstyle=|\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}|-|\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})=0\}|-|\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})=1\}|

(110)

\textstyle=n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)(2n_{1}^{2}+2n_{2}^{2}+16n_{1}n_{2}-34n_{1}-34n_{2}+60).

(111)

Since
\psi_{YZ,2}
upper-bounds
\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}
and
n\geq 4
, the following estimate, provided by
105
and
111
, completes our proof:

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,n}|\mathbb{X})]

\textstyle\leq\frac{2\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}(n_{1}^{2}+n_{2}^{2}+8n_{1}n_{2}-17(n_{1}+n_{2})+30)}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\leq\frac{\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}(2n_{1}^{2}+2n_{2}^{2}+16n_{1}n_{2})}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}.

(112)

B.2.2
Proof of
98

In this case, we can write

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X})]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s})^{2}|\mathbb{X}]]-\mathbb{E}[(\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X}])^{2}]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}^{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}}\sum_{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad\ \mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad\ -\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big],\end{split}

where
\tilde{\pi}_{i}\triangleq m\pi_{\lfloor i/m\rfloor}+i-m\lfloor i/m\rfloor
.
We now express
\mathsf{I}_{\text{total}}
as the disjoint union of four sets:

•

\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{diff}}
, containing
s_{1}^{2}(s_{1}-1)^{2}s_{2}^{2}(s_{2}-1)^{2}m^{8}=s_{1}^{2}(s_{1}^{2}-2s_{1}+1)s_{2}^{2}(s_{2}^{2}-2s_{2}+1)m^{8}
indices for which the bins of each component are pairwise different.

•

\mathsf{I}_{a}
, containing the indices for which one or more of the pairs among
(i_{1},i_{2})
,
(i^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{2})
,
(n_{1}+j_{1},n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1})
,
(n_{1}+j_{2},n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2})
share the same bin, but
\{\lfloor i_{1}/m\rfloor,\lfloor i_{2}/m\rfloor\}\cap\{\lfloor i^{\prime}_{1}/m\rfloor,\lfloor i^{\prime}_{2}/m\rfloor\}=\emptyset
,
\{\lfloor(n_{1}+j_{1})/m\rfloor,\lfloor(n_{1}+j_{2})/m\rfloor\}\cap\{\lfloor(n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1})/m\rfloor,
and
\lfloor(n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2})/m\rfloor\}=\emptyset
.

•

\mathsf{I}_{b}
, containing the indices for which exactly one pair among
(i_{1},i_{2})
,
(i^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{2})
,
(n_{1}+j_{1},n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1})
,
(n_{1}+j_{2},n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2})
shares the same bin, and
|\{\lfloor i_{1}/m\rfloor,\lfloor i_{2}/m\rfloor\}\cap\{\lfloor i^{\prime}_{1}/m\rfloor,\lfloor i^{\prime}_{2}/m\rfloor\}|+|\{\lfloor(n_{1}+j_{1})/m\rfloor,\lfloor(n_{1}+j_{2})/m\rfloor\}\cap\{\lfloor(n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1})/m\rfloor,\lfloor(n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2})/m\rfloor\}|=1
.

•

\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
, containing the rest of indices. For indices in
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
, there has to be at least one pair among
(i_{1},i_{2})
,
(i^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{2})
,
(n_{1}+j_{1},n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1})
,
(n_{1}+j_{2},n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2})
that shares the same bin, and there have to be at least two pairs formed by an index in
\{i_{1},i_{2},n_{1}+j_{1},n_{1}+j_{2}\}
and an index in
\{i^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{2},n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1},n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}\}
such that both indices belong to the same bin. From this argument, we can deduce that

\textstyle|\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}|

\textstyle\leq(4s_{1}s_{2}^{4}+4s_{1}^{2}s_{2}^{3}+16s_{1}^{2}s_{2}^{3}+16s_{1}^{3}s_{2}^{2}+4s_{1}^{4}s_{2}+4s_{1}^{3}s_{2}^{2})m^{8}

(113)

\textstyle=(4s_{1}s_{2}^{4}+20s_{1}^{2}s_{2}^{3}+20s_{1}^{3}s_{2}^{2}+4s_{1}^{4}s_{2})m^{8}.

(114)

Contribution of
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{diff}}
terms

To deal with the contribution from
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{diff}}
terms, we mirror the argument of
[
31
, Thm. 4.1]
. In
Sec.

A.1
we have seen that cheap permutation can be treated like standard permutation of dataset bins with the substitutions
n\leftarrow s
,
n_{1}\leftarrow s_{1}
,
n_{2}\leftarrow s_{2}
,
h_{\mathrm{ho}}\leftarrow H_{\mathrm{ho},m}
,
g\leftarrow G_{m}
. To this end, we define the index sets

\textstyle\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}

\textstyle\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{8}_{+}:(i_{1},i_{2})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{s_{1}},(j_{1},j_{2})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{s_{2}},(i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{s_{1}},(j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{i}_{2}^{s_{2}}\},

(115)

\textstyle\mathsf{K}_{1}

\textstyle\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})\leq 1\},\ \ \mathsf{K}_{1}^{c}=\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})>1\},\ \ \text{and}\ \ \mathsf{K}_{2}\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})=2\}.

(116)

We compute the size of
\mathsf{K}_{2}
:

\textstyle|\mathsf{K}_{2}|

\textstyle=2s_{1}(s_{1}-1)s_{2}(s_{2}-1)(s_{2}-2)(s_{2}-3)+2s_{1}(s_{1}-1)(s_{1}-2)(s_{1}-3)s_{2}(s_{2}-1)

(117)

\textstyle\qquad+4s_{1}(s_{1}-1)(s_{1}-2)\cdot 4s_{2}(s_{2}-1)(s_{2}-2)

(118)

\textstyle=s_{1}(s_{1}-1)s_{2}(s_{2}-1)(2s_{2}^{2}+2s_{1}^{2}+16s_{1}s_{2}-42s_{1}-42s_{2}+88).

(119)

Hence, we obtain that

\textstyle|\mathsf{K}_{1}^{c}\setminus\mathsf{K}_{2}|

\textstyle=|\mathsf{K}_{1}^{c}|-|\mathsf{K}_{2}|=s_{1}(s_{1}-1)s_{2}(s_{2}-1)((-34s_{1}-34s_{2}+60)-(-42s_{1}-42s_{2}+88))

(120)

\textstyle=s_{1}(s_{1}-1)s_{2}(s_{2}-1)(8s_{1}+8s_{2}-28).

(121)

Our goal is now to upper-bound the contribution of
\mathsf{I}_{\text{diff}}
to
Sec.

B.2.2
,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\sum_{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{\text{diff}}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=m^{8}\sum_{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{total}}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{X}_{n}^{(\pi_{i_{1}})},\mathbb{X}_{n}^{(\pi_{i_{2}})};\mathbb{X}_{n}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{1}})},\mathbb{X}_{n}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{2}})})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{X}_{n}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{1}})},\mathbb{X}_{n}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{2}})};\mathbb{X}_{n}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}})},X_{n}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}})})\big|\mathbb{X}\big]\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sum_{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{total}}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},\ell_{1},\ell_{2}=1}^{m}\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{k_{1}}^{(\pi_{i_{1}})},X_{k_{2}}^{(\pi_{i_{2}})};X_{\ell_{1}}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{1}})},X_{\ell_{2}}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{2}})})\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\big(\sum_{k^{\prime}_{1},k^{\prime}_{2},\ell^{\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{m}\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{k^{\prime}_{1}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{1}})},X_{k^{\prime}_{2}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{2}})};X_{\ell^{\prime}_{1}}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}})},X_{\ell^{\prime}_{2}}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}})})\big)\big|\mathbb{X}\big]\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\sum_{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\mathrm{total}}}\sum_{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{\mathrm{total}}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{k_{1}}^{(\pi_{i_{1}})},X_{k_{2}}^{(\pi_{i_{2}})};X_{\ell_{1}}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{1}})},X_{\ell_{2}}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j_{2}})})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{k^{\prime}_{1}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{1}})},X_{k^{\prime}_{2}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{2}})};X_{\ell^{\prime}_{1}}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}})},X_{\ell^{\prime}_{2}}^{(\pi_{s_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}})})\big|\mathbb{X}\big]\big],\end{split}

where we define
\mathfrak{K}\triangleq(k_{1},k_{2},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},k^{\prime}_{1},k^{\prime}_{2},\ell^{\prime}_{1},\ell^{\prime}_{2})
,

\textstyle\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}

\textstyle\triangleq\{1,\dots,m\}^{8},\quad\mathsf{J}_{1}\triangleq\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})\leq 1\},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{J}_{1}^{c}=\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})>1\}.

(122)

Each non-zero summand in the final expression of
Sec.

B.2.2
has indices satisfying

1.

\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{2}
, with, for the indices in
\mathfrak{i}
that are equal, the corresponding indices in
\mathfrak{K}
are also equal (so that, for each
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{2}
, there are at most
m^{6}
terms with non-zero contribution), or

2.

\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{1}^{c}\setminus\mathsf{K}_{2}
, and
\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{1}^{c}
(as otherwise the terms would have zero contribution).

We now bound the size of
\mathsf{J}_{1}^{c}
:

\textstyle|\mathsf{J}_{1}|

\textstyle=|\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})=0\}|+|\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})=1\}|

(123)

\textstyle|\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})=0\}|

\textstyle\geq m(m-1)(m-2)(m-3)m(m-1)(m-2)(m-3)

(124)

\textstyle\quad+4m(m-1)(m-2)m(m-1)(m-2)(m-3)

(125)

\textstyle=m(m-1)(m-2)m(m-1)(m-2)(m-3)\big((m-3)+4\big)

(126)

\textstyle|\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})=1\}|

\textstyle\geq 8m(m-1)(m-2)m(m-1)(m-2)(m-3)

(127)

\textstyle\quad\Longrightarrow\quad|\mathsf{J}_{1}^{c}|

\textstyle=|\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}|-|\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})=0\}|-|\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}\,|\,\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})=1\}|

(128)

\textstyle\leq m^{8}-m(m-1)(m-2)m(m-1)(m-2)(m-3)\big((m-3)+4\big)

(129)

\textstyle\quad-8m(m-1)(m-2)m(m-1)(m-2)(m-3)

(130)

\textstyle=50m^{6}-228m^{5}+419m^{4}-348m+108\leq 50m^{6}.

(131)

Since every non-zero summand is bounded by
\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}
, we can bound
Sec.

B.2.2
by

\textstyle|\mathsf{K}_{2}|\cdot m^{6}\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}+|\mathsf{K}_{1}^{c}\setminus\mathsf{K}_{2}|\cdot|\mathsf{J}_{1}^{c}|\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}

(132)

\textstyle\leq s_{1}(s_{1}-1)s_{2}(s_{2}-1)((2s_{2}^{2}+2s_{1}^{2}+16s_{1}s_{2})m^{6}+(8s_{1}+8s_{2})\cdot 50m^{6})\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}

(133)

\textstyle=n_{1}(n_{1}-m)n_{2}(n_{2}-m)(2n_{2}^{2}+2n_{1}^{2}+16n_{1}n_{2}+400(n^{2}_{1}+n^{2}_{2})/s)\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2},

(134)

which, in turn, bounds the contribution of
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{diff}}
to
Sec.

B.2.2
.

Contribution of
\mathsf{I}_{a}
terms

Without loss of generality, assume that
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
belong to the same bin but that all other indices belong to pairwise different bins. Then,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]

(135)

\textstyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]

(136)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]

(137)

\textstyle\quad-\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]=0.

(138)

Contribution of
\mathsf{I}_{b}
terms

Without loss of generality, assume that
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
belong to the same bin and that
n_{1}+j_{1}
and
n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}
also belong to the same bin.
Since exchanging the indices
i^{\prime}_{1}
and
n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}
does not change the distribution, and
\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(x,y;x^{\prime},y^{\prime})=-\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(x^{\prime},y;x,y^{\prime})
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]

(139)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]

(140)

\textstyle=-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]=0.

(141)

The same argument works to show that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]=0.

(142)

Contribution of
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
terms

We consider further subcases.

\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
terms, subcase (i)

For indices in
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
such that
i_{1}
,
i_{2}
,
n_{1}+j_{1}
,
n_{1}+j_{2}
,
i^{\prime}_{1}
,
i^{\prime}_{2}
,
n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}
,
n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}
are all pairwise different,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]

(143)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\pi]\big]

(144)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\pi]\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\pi]\big].

(145)

Here,
\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\pi]
and
\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\pi]
play the same role, so we will focus on the latter. We consider the following cases:

•

\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}>n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}>n_{1}
or analogous configurations: Then,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\pi]=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{1},Y_{2};Z_{1},Z_{2})]=\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}],

(146)

•

\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}>n_{1}
or analogous configurations: Then,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\pi]=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{1},Y_{2};Y_{3},Z_{1})]

(147)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{3},Y_{2};Y_{1},Z_{1})]=-\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{1},Y_{2};Y_{3},Z_{1})]=0.

(148)

•

\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}\leq n_{1}
or analogous configurations: Then,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|\pi]=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{1},Y_{2};Y_{3},Y_{4})]

(149)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{3},Y_{2};Y_{1},Y_{4})]=-\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{1},Y_{2};Y_{3},Y_{4})]=0.

(150)

Thus, for all terms in subcase (i), we have that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]\leq\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}.

(151)

Moreover, the fraction of
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
terms in subcase (i) is
m^{2}(m-1)^{2}(m-2)^{2}(m-3)^{2}/m^{8}
.

\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
terms, subcase (ii)

Now we consider indices in
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
such that there is exactly one pair among
i_{1}
,
i_{2}
,
n_{1}+j_{1}
,
n_{1}+j_{2}
,
i^{\prime}_{1}
,
i^{\prime}_{2}
,
n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}
,
n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}
that are the same, and the other indices are pairwise different. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
i_{1}=i^{\prime}_{1}
(recall that
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
always). Then,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]

(152)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\pi]\big]

(153)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]

(154)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]|\pi]\big].

(155)

Here,
\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]
and
\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]
also play the same role, so we will focus on the latter. We consider the following cases with
(Y,Y^{\prime},Y^{\prime\prime},Z,Z^{\prime},Z^{\prime\prime})\sim\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}\times\mathbb{Q}\times\mathbb{Q}
given
(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi)
:

•

\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}>n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}>n_{1}
or analogous configurations: Then,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi].

(156)

•

\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}>n_{1}
or analogous configurations: Then,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y;Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi].

(157)

•

\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}>n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}\leq n_{1}
or analogous configurations: Then,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y;Z^{\prime},Y^{\prime})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]

(158)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y^{\prime};Z^{\prime},Y)|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]=-\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y;Z^{\prime},Y^{\prime})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]=0.

(159)

•

\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}\leq n_{1}
,
\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}\leq n_{1}
or analogous configurations: Then,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y;Y^{\prime},Y^{\prime\prime})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]

(160)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y^{\prime\prime};Y^{\prime},Y)|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]=-\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y;Y^{\prime},Y^{\prime\prime})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]=0.

(161)

Thus, we obtain that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]

(162)

\textstyle\leq\max\big\{\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]^{2}]\big],\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},Y;Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})|X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},\pi]^{2}]\big]\big\}

(163)

\textstyle=\max\big\{\frac{n_{1}}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y^{\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|Y^{\prime}]^{2}\big]+\frac{n_{2}}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Z^{\prime\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|Z^{\prime\prime}]^{2}\big],

(164)

\textstyle\qquad\quad\frac{n_{1}}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y^{\prime\prime},Y;Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})|Y^{\prime\prime}]^{2}\big]+\frac{n_{2}}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Z^{\prime\prime},Y;Y^{\prime},Z)|Z^{\prime}]^{2}\big]\big\}.

(165)

Now, notice that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y^{\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|Y^{\prime}]^{2}\big]

\textstyle=\mathrm{Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y^{\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|Y^{\prime}]\big)+\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y^{\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})]^{2}

(166)

\textstyle=\mathrm{Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y^{\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|Y^{\prime}]\big)+\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2},

(167)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Z^{\prime\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|Z^{\prime\prime}]^{2}\big]

\textstyle=\mathrm{Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Z^{\prime\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|Z^{\prime\prime}]\big)+\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Z^{\prime\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})]^{2}

(168)

\textstyle=\mathrm{Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Z^{\prime\prime},Y;Z,Z^{\prime})|Z^{\prime\prime}]\big),

(169)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y^{\prime\prime},Y;Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})|Y^{\prime\prime}]^{2}\big]

\textstyle=\mathrm{Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y^{\prime\prime},Y;Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})|Y^{\prime\prime}]\big),\quad\text{and}\quad

(170)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Z^{\prime\prime},Y;Y^{\prime},Z^{\prime})|Z^{\prime\prime}]^{2}\big]

\textstyle=\mathrm{Var}\big(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y^{\prime},Y;Z^{\prime\prime},Z^{\prime})|Z^{\prime\prime}]\big)+\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}.

(171)

Thus, for
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
terms in subcase (ii),

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]

(172)

\textstyle\leq\max\{\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\}+\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}.

(173)

Moreover, the fraction of
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
terms in this subcase is upper-bounded by
12m^{2}(m-1)^{2}(m-2)^{2}(m-3)/m^{8}
.

\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
terms, subcase (iii)

For the remaining terms in
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
we can write

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]\big]\leq\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}.

(174)

Since there need to be at least two pairs of indices that share the same value, we have that the fraction of elements of
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
that fall under subcase (iii) is upper-bounded by

\textstyle\frac{1}{m^{8}}\big({4\choose 3}m^{6}+{4\choose 3}m^{6}+{4\choose 2}\cdot{4\choose 2}m^{6}\big)=\frac{44}{m^{2}}.

(175)

Total contribution of
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
terms

We end up with a bound of the form

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\sum_{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{I}_{\text{diff}}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j_{2}}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{i^{\prime}_{2}}};X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{1}}},X_{\tilde{\pi}_{n_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}}})|\mathbb{X}]]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq|\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}|\big(\frac{(m-1)^{2}(m-2)^{2}(m-3)^{2}}{m^{6}}\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\quad+\frac{12(m-1)^{2}(m-2)^{2}(m-3)}{m^{6}}\big(\max\{\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\}+\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}\big)+\frac{44}{m^{2}}\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq(4s_{1}s_{2}^{4}+20s_{1}^{2}s_{2}^{3}+20s_{1}^{3}s_{2}^{2}+4s_{1}^{4}s_{2})m^{8}\big(\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}+\frac{12}{m}\max\{\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\}+\frac{44}{m^{2}}\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}\big).\end{split}

Combining our
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{diff}}
,
\mathsf{I}_{a},

\mathsf{I}_{b},
and
\mathsf{I}_{\mathrm{rest}}
estimates, we obtain the advertised variance bound,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X})]\,n_{1}^{2}(n_{1}-1)^{2}n_{2}^{2}(n_{2}-1)^{2}

(176)

\textstyle\leq\big(n_{1}(n_{1}-m)n_{2}(n_{2}-m)(2n_{2}^{2}+2n_{1}^{2}+16n_{1}n_{2}+400(n^{2}_{1}+n^{2}_{2})/s)\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}

(177)

\textstyle+(4n_{1}n_{2}^{4}+20n_{1}^{2}n_{2}^{3}+20n_{1}^{3}n_{2}^{2}+4n_{1}^{4}n_{2})m^{3}\big(\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}+\frac{12}{m}\max\{\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\}+\frac{44}{m^{2}}\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}\big)\big)

(178)

\textstyle\leq\big((n_{1}-m)(n_{2}-m)(2n_{2}^{2}+2n_{1}^{2}+16n_{1}n_{2}+400(n^{2}_{1}+n^{2}_{2})/s)\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}

(179)

\textstyle+(4n_{2}^{3}+20n_{1}n_{2}^{2}+20n_{1}^{2}n_{2}+4n_{1}^{3})m^{3}\big(\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}+\frac{12}{m}\max\{\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\}+\frac{44}{m^{2}}\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}\big)\big)

(180)

\textstyle\leq\big((n_{1}-m)(n_{2}-m)(2n_{2}^{2}+2n_{1}^{2}+16n_{1}n_{2}+400(n^{2}_{1}+n^{2}_{2})/s)\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}

(181)

\textstyle+8n^{3}m^{3}\big(\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]^{2}+\frac{12}{m}\max\{\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\}+\frac{44}{m^{2}}\tilde{\psi}_{YZ,2}\big)\big).

(182)

Appendix C
Proof of

Corollary

2
:
Corollary 2

(Power of cheap homogeneity: finite variance, PD)
.

We begin with lemma that relates the parameters of
Corollary

2
to those of
Thm.

1
.

Lemma 3

(Positive-definite bounds on
\psi_{Y,1}
,
\psi_{Z,1}
)
.

Under the premises of
Corollary

2
,

\textstyle\psi_{Y,1}\leq\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\xi_{\mathbb{Q}}\quad\text{and}\quad\psi_{Z,1}\leq\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\xi_{\mathbb{P}}.

(183)

Proof.

We prove one case as the others are identical.
By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle|\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{1},Y_{2};Z_{1},Z_{2})|Z_{1}]|=|\mathbb{E}[h_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{1},Y_{2};Z_{1},Z_{2})|Z_{1}]|\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=|\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{k}(Y_{1},Y_{2})+\mathbf{k}(Z_{1},Z_{2})-\mathbf{k}(Y_{1},Z_{2})-\mathbf{k}(Y_{2},Z_{1})|Z_{1}]|\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\big|\int\mathbf{k}(x,x^{\prime})\,d(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q})(x)\,d(\mathbb{P}-\delta_{Z_{1}})(x^{\prime})\big|\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\big|\langle{\int\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)\,d(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q})(x)},{\int\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)\,d(\mathbb{P}-\delta_{Z_{1}})(x)}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}\big|\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\|{\int\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)\,d(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q})(x)}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\|{\int\mathbf{k}(x,\cdot)\,d(\mathbb{P}-\delta_{Z_{1}})(x)}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Z_{1}},\mathbb{P}).\end{split}

Since
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{ho}}(Y_{1},Y_{2};Z_{1},Z_{2})|Z_{1}]]=0
, squaring and taking expectations yields the claim.
∎

We first show that
55
implies the sufficient power condition
35
of
Thm.

1
.
Since

\textstyle\sqrt{\frac{3-\beta}{\beta}\big(\frac{4\psi_{Y,1}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\psi_{Z,1}}{n_{2}}\big)}\leq\sqrt{\frac{3-\beta}{\beta}\big(\frac{4\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\xi_{\mathbb{Q}}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\xi_{\mathbb{P}}}{n_{2}}\big)}

(184)

by
Lem.

3
and
\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})

by
[
25
, Lemma 6]
, the following inequality is a sufficient condition for
35
to hold:

\textstyle\qquad ax^{2}-bx-c\geq 0\quad\text{for}\quad x=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q}),

(185)

\textstyle a=1,\ \ b=\sqrt{\frac{3-\beta}{\beta}\big(\frac{4\xi_{\mathbb{Q}}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\xi_{\mathbb{P}}}{n_{2}}\big)},\ \ \text{and}\ \ c=\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha^{\star}}{\alpha^{\star}}\frac{(36n_{1}^{2}+36n_{2}^{2}+198n_{1}n_{2})\psi_{YZ,2}}{\beta n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}}.

(186)

By the quadratic formula, this holds whenever

x\geq b+\sqrt{c}
,
yielding the first claim.

We next show that
54
implies the sufficient power condition
34
of
Thm.

1
. Let
f=\sqrt{s^{3}\frac{\beta\alpha^{\star}}{24(1-\alpha^{\star})}\rho_{n_{1}n_{2}}}
.
Using the equality
\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})
and the inequalities

\textstyle\sqrt{\frac{201s^{2}}{4n^{2}}\psi_{YZ,2}+{\frac{12s}{n}\max(\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1})}{}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{201s^{2}}{4n^{2}}\psi_{YZ,2}}+\sqrt{{\frac{12s}{n}\max(\xi_{\mathbb{Q}},\xi_{\mathbb{P}})}{}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})

(187)

and
184

justified by
Lem.

3
and the triangle inequality, we find that
34
holds whenever

\textstyle a

\textstyle x^{2}-bx-c\geq 0\quad\text{for}\quad x=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q}),\quad a=1-1/f,

(188)

\textstyle b

\textstyle=\sqrt{\frac{3-\beta}{\beta}\big(\frac{4\xi_{\mathbb{Q}}}{n_{1}}+\frac{4\xi_{\mathbb{P}}}{n_{2}}\big)}+\frac{1}{f}\sqrt{\frac{12s}{n}\max(\xi_{\mathbb{Q}},\xi_{\mathbb{P}})},\quad\text{and}\quad

(189)

\textstyle c

\textstyle=\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha^{\star}}{\alpha^{\star}}\frac{(36n_{1}^{2}+36n_{2}^{2}+198n_{1}n_{2})\psi_{YZ,2}}{\beta n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}}+\frac{1}{f}\sqrt{\frac{201s^{2}}{4n^{2}}\psi_{YZ,2}}.

(190)

Since
a\leq 1
, this holds when
x\geq\frac{b}{a}+\frac{\sqrt{c}}{a}\geq\frac{b}{a}+\sqrt{\frac{c}{a}}
, yielding the second claim.

Appendix D
Proof of

Thm.

3
:
Theorem 3

(Power of cheap homogeneity: sub-Gaussian, PD)
.

We will establish the claim using the quantile comparison method (
Proposition

2
) by identifying complementary quantile bounds (CQBs)
\Phi_{n_{1},n_{2}},\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s},
and
\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}
satisfying the tail bounds
42
,
43
, and
44
for the test statistic
U_{n_{1},n_{2}}
, auxiliary sequence
(U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi_{b},s})_{b=1}^{B}
, and population parameter
\mathcal{T}=\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})
.
Throughout, we will call a random variable
sub-Gaussian
if it has finite sub-Gaussian norm

\textstyle\|X\|_{\psi_{2}}\triangleq\sup_{p\geq 1}\frac{(\mathbb{E}[|X|^{p}])^{1/p}}{\sqrt{p}}.

(191)

D.1
Bounding the fluctuations of the test statistic

We begin by identifying a CQB
\Phi_{n_{1},n_{2}}
satisfying the test statistic tail bound
42
.
We first introduce another way to express the U-statistic
U_{n_{1},n_{2}}
and its corresponding V-statistic
V_{n_{1},n_{2}}
. If we let
\delta_{i}=1
for
i\in[n_{1}]
and
\delta_{i}=-1
for
i\in n_{1}+[n_{2}]
and
K_{ij}=g(X_{i},X_{j})
, we can write

\textstyle V_{n_{1},n_{2}}

\textstyle\triangleq\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=1]}{n_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=-1,\delta_{j}=-1]}{n_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}\delta_{j}=-1]}{n_{1}n_{2}}\big)K_{ij},

(192)

\textstyle U_{n_{1},n_{2}}

\textstyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=1,i\neq j]}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}+\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=-1,\delta_{j}=-1,i\neq j]}{n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}-\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}\delta_{j}=-1]}{n_{1}n_{2}}\big)K_{ij}.

(193)

Hence,

\textstyle V_{n_{1},n_{2}}\!-\!U_{n_{1},n_{2}}

\textstyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=1]}{n_{1}^{2}}\!+\!\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=-1,\delta_{j}=-1]}{n_{2}^{2}}\!-\!\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=1,i\neq j]}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}\!-\!\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=-1,\delta_{j}=-1,i\neq j]}{n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}\big)K_{ij}

(194)

\textstyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=1]}{n_{1}^{2}}\!+\!\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=-1]}{n_{2}^{2}})K_{ii}\!-\!\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{n}\big(\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=1,\delta_{j}=1]}{n_{1}^{2}(n_{1}-1)}\!+\!\frac{\mathrm{I}[\delta_{i}=-1,\delta_{j}=-1]}{n_{2}^{2}(n_{2}-1)}\big)K_{ij}

(195)

where we used that
\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}}-\frac{1}{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}=-\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}(n_{1}-1)}
.

Our proof makes use of two lemmas. The first, essentially due to
Maurer and Pontil [43]
, shows that functions of independent variables concentrate around their mean when coordinatewise function differences are sub-Gaussian.

Lemma 4

(Sub-Gaussian differences inequality
[
43
, Sec. 5]
)
.

Suppose an integrable function
f
of a sequence of independent datapoints
\mathbb{X}=(X_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
satisfies

\textstyle|f(\mathbb{X})-\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbb{X})\mid\mathbb{X}_{-k}]|\leq f_{k}(X_{k})\quad\text{almost surely}\quad

(196)

for each
k\in[n]
and measurable functions
(f_{k})_{k=1}^{n}
.
If each
f_{k}(X_{k})
is sub-Gaussian, then

\textstyle f(\mathbb{X})-\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbb{X})]<\sqrt{64e\log(1/\delta)\sum_{k\in[n]}\|f_{k}(X_{k})\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}}\quad\text{with probability at least}\quad 1-\delta.

(197)

Proof.

The proof of Thm. 3 in Sec. 5 of
[
43
]
shows that

\textstyle\mathrm{Pr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(f(\mathbb{X})-\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbb{X})]\geq\varepsilon}}\right)\leq\exp(16e\beta^{2}\sum_{k\in[n]}\|f_{k}(X_{k})\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}-\beta\varepsilon)\ \ \text{for all}\ \ \varepsilon,\beta>0.

(198)

Minimizing over
\beta>0
yields the result.
1
1

1

The statement of
[
43
, Thm. 3]
contains the apparent typo
32
in place of
64
, presumably due to incorrect minimization of
198
.

∎

The second lemma, proved in
Sec.

D.4
, uses
Lem.

4
to
derive suitable CQBs for
U_{n_{1},n_{2}}
and
V_{n_{1},n_{2}}
.

Lemma 5

(Sub-Gaussian homogeneity quantile bounds)
.

Under the assumptions of
Thm.

3
,
for
V_{n_{1},n_{2}}\triangleq\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})
and each
\beta\in(0,1)
,

\textstyle\Pr\big(|\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}

\textstyle(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})-V_{n_{1},n_{2}}|\geq\Lambda_{V_{n_{1},n_{2}}}^{2}(\beta)+2\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\Lambda_{V_{n_{1},n_{2}}}(\beta)\big)\leq\beta\quad\text{and}

(199)

\textstyle\Pr\big(|\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}

\textstyle(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})-U_{n_{1},n_{2}}|\geq\Phi_{n_{1},n_{2}}(\beta)\big)\leq\beta\quad\text{where}

(200)

\textstyle\Lambda_{V_{n_{1},n_{2}}}(\beta)

\textstyle\triangleq\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{P}}}{n_{1}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{Q}}}{n_{2}}}+\sqrt{64e\log(\frac{1}{\beta})\big(\frac{\sigma_{\mathbb{P}}^{2}}{n_{1}}+\frac{\sigma_{\mathbb{Q}}^{2}}{n_{2}}\big)}

(201)

\textstyle\Lambda_{U_{n_{1},n_{2}}}(\beta)

\textstyle\triangleq\sqrt{\frac{2\,\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{P}}}{n_{1}-1}}+\sqrt{\frac{2\,\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{Q}}}{n_{2}-1}}+\sqrt{128e\log(\frac{2}{\beta})(\frac{(n_{1}+1)\sigma_{\mathbb{P}}^{2}}{n_{1}^{2}}+\frac{(n_{2}+1)\sigma_{\mathbb{Q}}^{2}}{n_{2}^{2}})},\quad\text{and}

(202)

\textstyle\Phi_{n_{1},n_{2}}(\beta)

\textstyle\triangleq\Lambda_{U_{n_{1},n_{2}}}^{2}(\beta)+2\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\Lambda_{U_{n_{1},n_{2}}}(\beta).

(203)

D.2
Bounding the fluctuations of the threshold

We next identify CQBs
\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}
and
\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}
that satisfy the properties

\textstyle\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U^{\pi,s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\geq\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)\mid\mathbb{X}}}\right)\leq\alpha,\qquad\forall\alpha\in(0,1),\quad\text{and}\quad

(204)

\textstyle\Pr(\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)\geq\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}(\alpha,\beta))\leq\beta,\qquad\forall\alpha,\beta\in(0,1)

(205)

for the cheaply permuted U-statistic
U^{\pi,s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}

78
.
Our derivation makes use of three lemmas.
The first is tail bound for random quadratic forms known as the Hanson-Wright inequality.

Lemma 6

(Hanson-Wright inequality
[
14
, Thm. 3.1]
)
.

Let
X\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a random vector with independent components satisfying
\mathbb{E}[e^{\lambda X_{i}}]\leq e^{\lambda^{2}\nu^{2}/2}

for some
\nu\geq 0
, all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, and all
i\in[n]
.
Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be a symmetric matrix such that
a_{ii}=0
for
i\in[n]
. Then, there exists a universal constant
\mathfrak{c}>3/20
such that for any
t>0
,

\textstyle\mathrm{Pr}(X^{\top}AX>t)\leq\exp(-\mathfrak{c}\min\{\frac{t^{2}}{\nu^{4}\|{A}\|_{F}^{2}},\frac{t}{\nu^{2}\|{A}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}\}).

(206)

Equivalently, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\textstyle X^{\top}AX\leq\max\big\{\nu^{2}\|{A}\|_{F}\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{\mathfrak{c}}},\frac{\log(1/\delta)\nu^{2}\|{A}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathfrak{c}}\big\}.

(207)

Moreover, when
\delta\in(0,0.86)
, we have

\textstyle X^{\top}AX\leq\frac{\log(1/\delta)\nu^{2}\|{A}\|_{F}}{\mathfrak{c}}\leq\frac{20\log(1/\delta)\nu^{2}\|{A}\|_{F}}{3}.

(208)

Proof.

Equation
206
is the content of
[
14
, Thm. 3.1]
, and equivalence with
207
follows from selecting
t=\max\big\{\sqrt{a\log(1/\delta)},b\log(1/\delta)\big\}
in
206
or
\delta=\exp(-\min\{\frac{\mathfrak{c}t^{2}}{\nu^{4}\|{A}\|_{F}^{2}},\frac{\mathfrak{c}t}{\nu^{2}\|{A}\|_{\mathrm{op}}}\})
in
207
.
Now, suppose that
\delta\in(0,0.86)
. Since
0.86\leq\exp(-3/20)\leq\exp(-\mathfrak{c})
, we have
\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{\mathfrak{c}}}\leq\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{\mathfrak{c}}
.
Since
\|{A}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|{A}\|_{F}
, the claim
208
follows.
∎

Our second lemma, proved in
Sec.

D.5
, provides high-probability bounds on the sums of MMD powers.

Lemma 7

(High-probability bounds on sums of MMD powers)
.

Under the assumptions of
Thm.

3
, if
p\in\{1,2,4\}
, then

\textstyle Z_{p}(\mathbb{X})

\textstyle\triangleq\big(\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{p}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})+\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{p}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q})\big)^{1/p}

(209)

\textstyle<\mathcal{A}_{p}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\delta),

(210)

with probability at least
1-\delta
for

\textstyle\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\delta)

\textstyle\triangleq\sqrt{64e\log(1/\delta)\big(\frac{n_{1}\sigma_{\mathbb{P}}^{2}}{m^{2}}+\frac{n_{2}\sigma_{\mathbb{Q}}^{2}}{m^{2}}\big)},

(211)

\textstyle\mathcal{A}_{1}(s_{1},s_{2},m)

\textstyle\triangleq s_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\mathbb{P}}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Y},\mathbb{P})]}{m}}+s_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\mathbb{Q}}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Z},\mathbb{Q})]}{m}},

(212)

\textstyle\mathcal{A}_{2}(s_{1},s_{2},m)

\textstyle\triangleq\sqrt{\frac{s_{1}\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\mathbb{P}}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Y},\mathbb{P})]}{m}+\frac{s_{2}\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\mathbb{Q}}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Z},\mathbb{Q})]}{m}},\quad\text{and}\quad

(213)

\textstyle\mathcal{A}_{4}(s_{1},s_{2},m)

\textstyle\triangleq\big(\big(\frac{3}{m^{2}}+\frac{1}{m^{3}}\big)\big(s_{1}\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\mathbb{P}}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{Y},\mathbb{P})]+s_{2}\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\mathbb{Q}}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{Z},\mathbb{Q})]\big)\big)^{1/4}.

(214)

Our third lemma,
Lem.

8
, proved in
Sec.

D.6
, uses
Lems.

6
and
7
to
verify the CQB properties
204
and
205
for a particular pairing of
(\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s},\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s})
.

Lemma 8

(Sub-Gaussian quantile bound for permutation threshold)
.

Instantiate the assumptions and notation of
Lem.

7
.
For all
\alpha,\beta\in(0,1)
,

\textstyle\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}(\alpha,\beta)\triangleq\frac{20\log(2/\alpha)}{3s_{1}^{3/2}}\big(\sqrt{12}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\mathcal{A}_{2}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\beta/3))

(215)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{24}(\mathcal{A}_{4}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\beta/3))^{2}\big)

(216)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{2}{s_{1}^{2}}\big((\mathcal{A}_{2}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\beta/3))^{2}

(217)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\mathcal{A}_{1}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\beta/3))\big)

(218)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(2\log(4/\alpha)+1)}{s_{1}}.

(219)

satisfies
\Pr(\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)\geq\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}(\alpha,\beta))\leq\beta
for
\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)
defined in equation
289
and satisfying
\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U^{\pi,s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\geq\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)\mid\mathbb{X}}}\right)\leq\alpha
.
Here,
m\triangleq\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{s}
,
s_{1}\triangleq\frac{sn_{1}}{n_{1}+n_{2}}
, and
s_{2}\triangleq s-s_{1}
.

In the sequel, we will make use of a simplified version of
\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}
that, as an immediate corollary to
Lem.

8
, also satisfies the CQB property
205
.

Corollary 4

(Simplified Sub-Gaussian quantile bound for permutation threshold)
.

Instantiate the assumptions and notation of
Thm.

3
.
For all
\alpha,\beta\in(0,1)
,

\textstyle\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}(\alpha,\beta)

\textstyle\triangleq\frac{20\log(2/\alpha)}{3s_{1}}\big(\frac{\sqrt{12}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3}))}{\sqrt{m}}+\frac{\sqrt{24}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3})^{2})}{m}\big)

(220)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{2}{s_{1}}\big(\frac{2(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{2}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3})^{2})}{m}+\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\sqrt{2}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3}))}{\sqrt{ms_{1}}}\big)

(221)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(2\log(4/\alpha)+1)}{s_{1}}.

(222)

satisfies
\Pr(\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)\geq\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}(\alpha,\beta))\leq\beta

for
m
,
s_{1}
, and
\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}
as in
Lem.

8
.

D.3
Putting everything together

Fix any
\beta\in(0,1)
, and suppose that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]

\textstyle\geq\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}(\alpha^{\star},\beta/3)+\Phi_{n_{1},n_{2}}(\beta/3)

(223)

for
\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}
and
\Phi_{n_{1},n_{2}}
defined in
Corollaries

4
and
5
.
By
Corollary

4
,
Lem.

5
, and
Proposition

2
, a cheap permutation test based on
U_{n_{1},n_{2}}
has power at least
1-\beta
.
We now show that the assumed condition
59
is sufficient to imply
223
.
Since
\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})
, the condition
223
is equivalent to the inequality
ax^{2}-bx-c\geq 0
with
x=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})
,

\textstyle a

\textstyle=1-\frac{2\log(4/\alpha^{\star})+1}{s_{1}},

(224)

\textstyle b

\textstyle=\frac{40\sqrt{\log(2/\alpha^{\star})}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3}))}{\sqrt{3s_{1}n_{1}}}\!+\!\frac{2(\sqrt{2}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3}))}{s_{1}\sqrt{n_{1}}}\!+\!\frac{2\tilde{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{P}}(\beta)}{\sqrt{n_{1}-1}}\!+\!\frac{2\tilde{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\beta)}{\sqrt{n_{2}-1}},\quad\text{and}\quad

(225)

\textstyle c

\textstyle=\frac{40\sqrt{2\log(2/\alpha^{\star})/3}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3})^{2})+4(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{2}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3})^{2})}{n_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{P}}(\beta)}{\sqrt{n_{1}-1}}+\frac{\tilde{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\beta)}{\sqrt{n_{2}-1}}\bigg)^{2}.

(226)

By the triangle inequality and the fact
\sqrt{a}\geq a
,
it therefore suffices to have

\textstyle x\geq\frac{1}{a}(b+\sqrt{c})\geq\frac{b}{a}+\sqrt{\frac{c}{a}}\geq\frac{b+\sqrt{b^{2}+4ac}}{2a}.

(227)

The advertised result now follows from the observation that

\textstyle\sqrt{c}\leq\sqrt{\frac{40\sqrt{2\log(2/\alpha^{\star})/3}(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{4}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3})^{2})+4(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{2}+\tilde{\mathcal{B}}(\frac{\beta}{3})^{2})}{n_{1}}}+\frac{\tilde{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{P}}(\beta)}{\sqrt{n_{1}-1}}+\frac{\tilde{\Lambda}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\beta)}{\sqrt{n_{2}-1}}.

(228)

D.4
Proof of

Lem.

5
:
Lemma 5

(Sub-Gaussian homogeneity quantile bounds)
.

Fix any
\beta\in(0,1)
.
Let
\mathbb{X}
be the concatenation of
\mathbb{Y}
and
\mathbb{Z}
, and let
\tilde{Y}
and
\tilde{Z}
be independent draws from
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathbb{Q}
respectively.
By the triangle inequality, we have the following almost sure inequalities for each
k\in[n]
:

\textstyle|\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})-\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})|

\textstyle\leq\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\widehat{\mathbb{P}})+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{Q},\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})\triangleq\Delta(\mathbb{X})

(229)

\textstyle|\Delta(\mathbb{X})-\mathbb{E}[\Delta(\mathbb{X})\mid\mathbb{X}_{-k}]|

\textstyle\leq\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n_{1}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Y_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Y}})\mid Y_{k}]&\text{if\ }k\leq n_{1}\\
\frac{1}{n_{2}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Z_{k-n_{1}}},\delta_{\tilde{Z}})\mid Z_{k-n_{1}}]&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(230)

Furthermore, by Jensen’s inequality, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta(\mathbb{X})]

\textstyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\widehat{\mathbb{P}})^{2}]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{Q},\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})^{2}]}

(231)

\textstyle=\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[((\widehat{\mathbb{P}}-\mathbb{P})\times(\widehat{\mathbb{P}}-\mathbb{P}))\mathbf{k}]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[((\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}-\mathbb{Q})\times(\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}-\mathbb{Q}))\mathbf{k}]}

(232)

\textstyle=\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}((\delta_{Y_{i}}-\mathbb{P})\times(\delta_{Y_{i}}-\mathbb{P}))\mathbf{k}]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{n_{2}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{2}}((\delta_{Z_{i}}-\mathbb{Q})\times(\delta_{Z_{i}}-\mathbb{Q}))\mathbf{k}]}

(233)

\textstyle=\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{P}}}{n_{1}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{Q}}}{n_{2}}}.

(234)

Hence, by
Lem.

4
, with probability at least
1-\beta
,

\textstyle|\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})-\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})|<\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{P}}}{n_{1}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{Q}}}{n_{2}}}+\sqrt{64e\log(\frac{1}{\beta})(\frac{\sigma_{\mathbb{P}}^{2}}{n_{1}}+\frac{\sigma_{\mathbb{Q}}^{2}}{n_{2}})}=\Lambda_{V_{n_{1},n_{2}}}(\beta).

(235)

Since
|a^{2}-b^{2}|=|a-b||b+a|\leq|a-b|(|b-a|+2|a|)
for all real
a,b
, we further have

\textstyle|\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})-\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})|<\Lambda_{V_{n_{1},n_{2}}}^{2}(\beta)+2\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\Lambda_{V_{n_{1},n_{2}}}(\beta)

(236)

with probability at least
1-\beta
, as advertised.

To establish the
U_{n_{1},n_{2}}
bound, we begin by writing

\textstyle\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})-U_{n_{1},n_{2}}=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})-V_{n_{1},n_{2}}+V_{n_{1},n_{2}}-U_{n_{1},n_{2}}.\!\!

(237)

To bound the term
V_{n_{1},n_{2}}-U_{n_{1},n_{2}}
,
observe that, by
195
,

\textstyle V_{n_{1},n_{2}}-U_{n_{1},n_{2}}

\textstyle=\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Y_{i}},\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{-i}})+\frac{1}{n_{2}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Z_{i}},\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{-i}})\geq 0.

(238)

Hence, we can write

\textstyle\tilde{\Delta}(\mathbb{X})=\sqrt{V_{n_{1},n_{2}}-U_{n_{1},n_{2}}}=\big\|\big(\frac{1}{n_{1}}(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Y_{i}},\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{-i}}))_{i=1}^{n_{1}},\frac{1}{n_{2}}(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Z_{i}},\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{-i}}))_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\big)\big\|_{2}.

(239)

By repeated use of the triangle inequality, we have, for each
k\in[n_{1}]
,

\textstyle\big|\tilde{\Delta}(\mathbb{X})-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Delta}(\mathbb{X})|\mathbb{Y}_{-k},\mathbb{Z}]\big|\leq\frac{1}{n_{1}}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\big(\big(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Y_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Y}})\big)_{i=1,i\neq k}^{n_{1}},\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Y_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Y}})\big)\|_{2}|Y_{k}\big]

(240)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sqrt{1+\frac{n_{1}-1}{n_{1}^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Y_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Y}})|Y_{k}\big]\leq\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{n_{1}}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Y_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Y}})|Y_{k}\big]

(241)

almost surely.
Identical reasoning yields the following almost sure bound for each
k\in[n_{2}]
:

\textstyle\big|\tilde{\Delta}(\mathbb{X})-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Delta}(\mathbb{X})|\mathbb{Y},\mathbb{Z}_{-k}]\big|\leq\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{n_{2}}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{Z_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Z}})|Z_{k}\big].

(242)

Next, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we find that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Delta}(\mathbb{X})]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Delta}(\mathbb{X})^{2}]=\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Y_{i}},\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{-i}})+\frac{1}{n_{2}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Z_{i}},\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{-i}})\big],

(243)

where

\textstyle\mathbb{E}

\textstyle\big[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Y_{i}},\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{-i}})\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\iint k(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}(\delta_{Y_{i}}-\mathbb{P}+\mathbb{P}-\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{-i}})(x)\,\mathrm{d}(\delta_{Y_{i}}-\mathbb{P}+\mathbb{P}-\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{-i}})(y)\big]

(244)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[(1+\frac{1}{n_{1}-1})\iint k(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}(\delta_{Y_{i}}-\mathbb{P})(x)\,\mathrm{d}(\delta_{Y_{i}}-\mathbb{P})(y)\big]=\big(1+\frac{1}{n_{1}-1}\big)\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{P}}.

(245)

Analogously,
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{Z_{i}},\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{-i}})\big]=\big(1+\frac{1}{n_{2}-1}\big)\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{Q}}
.
Thus, we conclude that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\Delta}(\mathbb{X})]\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{P}}}{n_{1}-1}+\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{Q}}}{n_{2}-1}}.

(246)

Hence, applying
Lem.

4
, we obtain that with probability at least
1-\beta/2
,

\textstyle\sqrt{V_{n_{1},n_{2}}-U_{n_{1},n_{2}}}<\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{P}}}{n_{1}-1}+\frac{\mathrm{M}_{2,\mathbb{Q}}}{n_{2}-1}}+\sqrt{64e\log(\frac{2}{\beta})(\frac{(n_{1}+1)\sigma_{\mathbb{P}}^{2}}{n_{1}^{2}}+\frac{(n_{2}+1)\sigma_{\mathbb{Q}}^{2}}{n_{2}^{2}})}.

(247)

This estimate together with the union bound and the relations
236
and
237
yield the claim.

D.5
Proof of

Lem.

7
:
Lemma 7

(High-probability bounds on sums of MMD powers)
.

Define

\textstyle\phi(y)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{Y^{\prime}\sim\mathbb{P}}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{y},\delta_{Y^{\prime}})]\quad\text{and}\quad\phi^{\prime}(z)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{Z^{\prime}\sim\mathbb{Q}}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{z},\delta_{Z^{\prime}})],

(248)

fix any
p\in\{1,2,4\}
,
and note that we may write

\textstyle Z_{p}(\mathbb{X})=\big\|\big((\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P}))_{i=1}^{s_{1}},(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}))_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\big)\big\|_{p},\quad\text{and}\quad

(249)

\textstyle|Z_{p}(\mathbb{X})-\mathbb{E}[Z_{p}(\mathbb{X})\mid\mathbb{X}_{-k}]|\leq\begin{cases}\frac{1}{m}\phi(Y_{k})&\text{if\ }k\leq n_{1}\\
\frac{1}{m}\phi^{\prime}(Z_{k-n_{1}})&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(250)

almost surely by the triangle inequality.
Applying
Lem.

4

we therefore have

\textstyle Z_{p}(\mathbb{X})-\mathbb{E}[Z_{p}(\mathbb{X})]<\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\delta)\quad\text{with probability at least}\quad 1-\delta.

(251)

Hence, it remains to upper-bound
\mathbb{E}[Z_{p}(\mathbb{X})]
.
Applying Hölder’s inequality, we find that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[Z_{1}(\mathbb{X})]

\textstyle=\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})]+\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q})]

(252)

\textstyle=\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})]}+\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q})]}=\mathcal{A}_{1}(s_{1},s_{2},m),

(253)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[Z_{2}(\mathbb{X})]^{2}

\textstyle\leq\mathbb{E}[Z_{2}(\mathbb{X})^{2}]=\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})]+\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q})]

(254)

\textstyle=\mathcal{A}_{2}(s_{1},s_{2},m),\quad\text{and}\quad

(255)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[Z_{4}(\mathbb{X})]^{4}

\textstyle\leq\mathbb{E}[Z_{4}(\mathbb{X})^{4}]=\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})]+\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q})]

(256)

Now consider the shorthand
(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}}-\mathbb{P})(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}}-\mathbb{P})\mathbf{k}\triangleq\iint k(x,y)\,d(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}}-\mathbb{P})(x)\,d(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}}-\mathbb{P})(y)
.
To conclude our fourth moment bound, we notice that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})]

(257)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\big((\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}}-\mathbb{P})(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}}-\mathbb{P})\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}\big]

(258)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{1}{m^{4}}\sum_{k,l,k^{\prime},l^{\prime}=1}^{m}\big((\delta_{Y_{mi+k}}-\mathbb{P})(\delta_{Y_{mi+l}}-\mathbb{P})\mathbf{k}\big)\big((\delta_{Y_{mi+k^{\prime}}}-\mathbb{P})(\delta_{Y_{mi+l^{\prime}}}-\mathbb{P})\mathbf{k}\big)\big]

(259)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{1}{m^{4}}\big(\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\delta_{Y_{mi+k}}-\mathbb{P})(\delta_{Y_{mi+k}}-\mathbb{P})\mathbf{k}\big)\big(\sum_{l=1}^{m}(\delta_{Y_{mi+l}}-\mathbb{P})(\delta_{Y_{mi+l}}-\mathbb{P})\mathbf{k}\big)

(260)

\textstyle\quad+\frac{2}{m^{4}}\sum_{k,l=1}^{m}\big((\delta_{Y_{mi+k}}-\mathbb{P})(\delta_{Y_{mi+l}}-\mathbb{P})\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{m^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\big((\delta_{Y_{mi+k}}-\mathbb{P})(\delta_{Y_{mi+k}}-\mathbb{P})\mathbf{k}\big)^{2}\big]

(261)

\textstyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\mathbb{P}}\big[\big(\frac{1}{m^{2}}+\frac{2}{m^{2}}+\frac{1}{m^{3}}\big)\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{Y},\mathbb{P})\big]=\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\mathbb{P}}\big[\big(\frac{3}{m^{2}}+\frac{1}{m^{3}}\big)\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{Y},\mathbb{P})\big]

(262)

and the analogous result for
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{P})]
.
This completes the proof.

D.6
Proof of

Lem.

8
:
Lemma 8

(Sub-Gaussian quantile bound for permutation threshold)
.

Fix any
\alpha,\beta\in(0,1)
.
We begin by identifying
\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)
satisfying

\textstyle\Pr\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V^{\pi,s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\geq\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)\mid\mathbb{X}}}\right)\leq\alpha

(263)

where

V_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}
is the cheaply permuted V-statistic
77
.
This in turn will imply
204
as
