V_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}\geq U_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}

by the same argument
195
used to show that
V_{n_{1},n_{2}}\geq U_{n_{1},n_{2}}
.

We follow a structure similar to that in the proof of
[
31
, Thm. 6.1]
.
Our first step is to identify a second random variable that, conditioned on
\mathbb{X}
, has the same distribution as
V_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}
.
Let
\ell=(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{s_{1}})
be an
s_{1}
tuple drawn without replacement from
[s_{2}]
generated independently of
(\mathbb{X},\pi)
and
\zeta=(\zeta_{k})_{k=1}^{s_{1}}
be independent random variables that take values
-1
and
1
with equal probability, independently of
(\mathbb{X},\pi,\ell)
.
For
k_{1},k_{2}\in[s_{1}]
, define the random measures

\textstyle\mu_{k}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{P}&\text{if }\pi_{k}\leq s_{1}\\
\mathbb{Q}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{P}&\text{if }\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k^{\prime}}}\leq s_{1}\\
\mathbb{Q}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(264)

By construction,
(\mu_{k_{1}}
,
\mu_{k_{2}}
,
\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}
,
\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})
are population distributions of the respective sample points

(\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}
,
\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}
,
\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{1}}}}
,
\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}})
.
Next, let us define

\textstyle\widetilde{V}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,\ell,\zeta,s}\triangleq\frac{1}{s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\zeta_{k_{1}}\zeta_{k_{2}}H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}},\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}};\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{1}}}},\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}}),

(265)

where
H_{\mathrm{ho},m}
is defined in
Sec.

A.1
.
By
[
31
, Sec. 6.1]
, conditional on
\mathbb{X}
, the distribution of
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{V}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,\ell,\zeta,s}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi,\zeta]
matches that of
V_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,s}
.

Our goal is now to bound the tail of
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{V}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,\ell,\zeta,s}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi,\zeta]
.
To this end, we define

\textstyle a_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi,\ell)=\frac{1}{s_{1}^{2}}(H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}},\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}};\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{1}}}},\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}})-\langle\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k},\mu_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}),

(266)

for
1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq s_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell}=(a_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi,\ell))_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}
. We remark that

\textstyle\widetilde{V}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,\ell,\zeta,s}=\zeta^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell}\zeta+c_{\zeta,\pi,\ell},

(267)

\textstyle\text{where }c_{\zeta,\pi,\ell}\triangleq\frac{1}{s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\zeta_{k_{1}}\zeta_{k_{2}}\langle\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k},\mu_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}},

(268)

which means that
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{V}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,\ell,\zeta,s}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi,\zeta]=\zeta^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi]\zeta+\mathbb{E}[c_{\zeta,\pi,\ell}\mid\zeta,\pi]
.

Observe that
\langle\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k},\mu_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}
can only take on the values
0
and
\pm\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})
. To make this more precise, we can partition
[s_{1}]
into the following subsets:
\mathcal{A}=\{k\in[s_{1}]\mid\mu_{k}=\mathbb{P},\mu^{\prime}_{k}=\mathbb{P}\}
,
\mathcal{B}=\{k\in[s_{1}]\mid\mu_{k}=\mathbb{P},\mu^{\prime}_{k}=\mathbb{Q}\}
,
\mathcal{C}=\{k\in[s_{1}]\mid\mu_{k}=\mathbb{Q},\mu^{\prime}_{k}=\mathbb{P}\}
,
\mathcal{D}=\{k\in[s_{1}]\mid\mu_{k}=\mathbb{Q},\mu^{\prime}_{k}=\mathbb{Q}\}
.
We will also introduce the partition
\mathcal{G}\sqcup\mathcal{F}
,
2
2

2

\sqcup
denotes a disjoint union.
where
\mathcal{G}=\{k\in[s_{1}]\mid\mu_{k}=\mathbb{P}\}
and
\mathcal{F}=\{k\in[s_{1}]\mid\mu_{k}=\mathbb{Q}\}
. Observe that
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{A}\sqcup\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{C}\sqcup\mathcal{D}
and that the membership of
k
in
\mathcal{G}
or
\mathcal{F}
is determined by
\pi
, while its membership in
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{C}
, or
\mathcal{D}
depends on
\ell
as well.

In terms of indicator function notation,
we have

\textstyle\langle{\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}},{\mu_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}

\textstyle=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}}-\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{B}}),

(269)

which means that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[c_{\zeta,\pi,\ell}\mid\zeta,\pi]\!=\!\frac{1}{s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\zeta_{k_{1}}\zeta_{k_{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}}\!-\!\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{B}}\mid\pi\big]\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q}).

(270)

If we let

b_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi)\triangleq\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}}-\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{B}}\mid\pi\big]
,
we have that
b_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi)
takes the following values depending on the pair
(k_{1},k_{2})
:

\textstyle b_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi)=\begin{cases}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}}\mid\pi,k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{G},k_{1}\neq k_{2}\big]\triangleq b_{1}&\text{if }k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{G},k_{1}\neq k_{2}\\
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{B}}\mid\pi,k_{1}\in\mathcal{G}\big]\triangleq b_{2}&\text{if }k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{G},k_{1}=k_{2}\\
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}}\mid\pi,k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{F},k_{1}\neq k_{2}\big]\triangleq b_{3}&\text{if }k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{F},k_{1}\neq k_{2}\\
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{C}}\mid\pi,k_{1}\in\mathcal{F}\big]\triangleq b_{4}&\text{if }k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{F},k_{1}=k_{2}\\
-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{C}}\mid\pi,k_{1}\in\mathcal{G},k_{2}\in\mathcal{F}\big]\triangleq-b_{5}&\text{if }k_{1}\in\mathcal{G},k_{2}\in\mathcal{F}\\
-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}}\mid\pi,k_{1}\in\mathcal{F},k_{2}\in\mathcal{G}\big]\triangleq-b_{6}&\text{if }k_{1}\in\mathcal{F},k_{2}\in\mathcal{G}.\end{cases}

(271)

Given
\pi
, let
q_{\mathcal{G}}
be the number of indices
k
such that
1\leq k\leq s_{2}

and
\pi_{s_{1}+k}\leq s_{1}
, and analogously, let
q_{\mathcal{F}}
be the number of indices
k
such that
1\leq k\leq s_{2}
and
\pi_{s_{1}+k_{2}}>s_{1}
, which means that
q_{\mathcal{G}}+q_{\mathcal{F}}=s_{2}
.
Observe that
b_{1}=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}}\mid\pi,k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathcal{G},k_{1}\neq k_{2}\big]=\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}(q_{\mathcal{G}}-1)}{s_{2}(s_{2}-1)}=\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}}{s_{2}^{2}}-\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}(s_{2}-q_{\mathcal{G}})}{s^{2}_{2}(s_{2}-1)}=\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}}{s_{2}^{2}}-\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s^{2}_{2}(s_{2}-1)}
. This is because the index
k_{1}
belongs to
\mathcal{B}
with probability
\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}}{s_{2}}
, and then conditioned on this fact, the index
k_{2}
belongs to
\mathcal{B}
with probability
\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}-1}{s_{2}-1}
.
Similarly,
b_{5}=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{C}}\mid\pi,k_{1}\in\mathcal{G},k_{2}\in\mathcal{F}\big]=\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}(s_{2}-1)}=\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}^{2}}+\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}^{2}(s_{2}-1)}
. Through analogous arguments we find that

\textstyle b_{3}=\frac{q_{\mathcal{F}}^{2}}{s_{2}^{2}}-\frac{q_{\mathcal{F}}q_{\mathcal{G}}}{s^{2}_{2}(s_{2}-1)}\quad\text{and}\quad b_{6}=\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}^{2}}+\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}^{2}(s_{2}-1)}.

(272)

Therefore,

\textstyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\zeta_{k_{1}}\zeta_{k_{2}}b_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi)

(273)

\textstyle=\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}\big)^{2}b_{1}+\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}1\big)\big(b_{2}-b_{1}\big)+\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)^{2}b_{3}+\big(\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{F}}1\big)\big(b_{4}-b_{3}\big)

(274)

\textstyle\qquad-\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}\big)\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)b_{5}-\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}\big)\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)b_{6}

(275)

\textstyle=\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}\big)^{2}\big(\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}}{s_{2}^{2}}-\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s^{2}_{2}(s_{2}-1)}\big)+\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}1\big)\big(b_{2}-b_{1}\big)

(276)

\textstyle\qquad+\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)^{2}\big(\frac{q_{\mathcal{F}}^{2}}{s_{2}^{2}}-\frac{q_{\mathcal{F}}q_{\mathcal{G}}}{s^{2}_{2}(s_{2}-1)}\big)+\big(\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathcal{F}}1\big)\big(b_{4}-b_{3}\big)

(277)

\textstyle\qquad-\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}\big)\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)\big(\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}^{2}}+\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}^{2}(s_{2}-1)}\big)-\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}\big)\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)\big(\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}^{2}}+\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}^{2}(s_{2}-1)}\big)

(278)

\textstyle=\big(\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}\big)\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}}{s_{2}}-\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)\frac{q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}}\big)^{2}-\frac{q_{\mathcal{G}}q_{\mathcal{F}}}{s_{2}^{2}(s_{2}-1)}\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}+\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)^{2}

(279)

\textstyle\qquad+|\mathcal{G}|\big(b_{2}-b_{1}\big)+|\mathcal{F}|\big(b_{4}-b_{3}\big)

(280)

\textstyle\leq\big(\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{k\in\mathcal{B}}\mid\pi,k\in\mathcal{G}\big]-\big(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{k\in\mathcal{C}}\mid\pi,k\in\mathcal{F}\big]\big)^{2}+s_{1}.

(281)

Let
c_{1}=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{k\in\mathcal{B}}\mid\pi,k\in\mathcal{G}\big]
,
c_{2}=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{k\in\mathcal{C}}\mid\pi,k\in\mathcal{F}\big]
.
By Hoeffding’s inequality
[
29
, Thm. 2]
and the union bound

\textstyle\Pr\big(|c_{1}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}+c_{2}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}|\geq t\mid\pi\big)\leq 2\exp\big(-\frac{t^{2}}{2(c_{1}^{2}|\mathcal{G}|+c_{2}^{2}|\mathcal{F}|)}\big)\leq 2\exp\big(-\frac{t^{2}}{2s_{1}}\big).

(282)

Therefore, with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime\prime}
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[c_{\zeta,\pi,\ell}\mid\zeta,\pi]\!

\textstyle=\!\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})}{s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\zeta_{k_{1}}\zeta_{k_{2}}b_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi)

(283)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})}{s_{1}^{2}}\big(\big(c_{1}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{G}}\zeta_{k}+c_{2}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{F}}\zeta_{k}\big)^{2}+s_{1}\big)

(284)

\textstyle<\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})}{s_{1}^{2}}\big(2\log(2/\delta^{\prime\prime})s_{1}+s_{1}\big)=\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(2\log(2/\delta^{\prime\prime})+1)}{s_{1}}.

(285)

It remains to bound the tail of

\textstyle\zeta^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi]\zeta=\zeta^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi]\zeta+\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell})\mid\mathbb{X},\pi],

(286)

where
\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}
has the off-diagonal components of
\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell}
and zeros in the diagonal.
We bound
\zeta^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi]\zeta
first.
Since
\sup_{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}[e^{\lambda{\zeta}_{1}-\lambda^{2}/2}]\leq 1

[
7
, Lem. 2.2]
,
Lem.

6
implies that

\textstyle\zeta^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi]\zeta\leq\frac{20\log(1/\delta^{\prime})\|\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi]\|_{F}}{3}\leq\frac{20\log(1/\delta^{\prime})\sup_{\pi,\ell}\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}\|_{F}}{3}

(287)

with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}
conditioned on
(\mathbb{X},\pi)
, provided that
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,0.86)
.
Setting
\delta^{\prime\prime}=\frac{\alpha}{2}
and
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\alpha}{2}
, we find that with probability at least
1-\alpha
conditioned on
\mathbb{X}
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{V}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\pi,\ell,\zeta,s}

\textstyle\mid\mathbb{X},\pi,\zeta]=\zeta^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}\mid\mathbb{X},\pi]\zeta+\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell})\mid\mathbb{X},\pi]+\mathbb{E}[c_{\zeta,\pi,\ell}\mid\pi,\zeta]

(288)

\textstyle<\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)\!\triangleq\!\frac{20\log(\frac{2}{\alpha})\sup_{\pi,\ell}\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}\|_{F}}{3}\!+\!\sup_{\pi,\ell}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell})+\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(2\log(4/\alpha)+1)}{s_{1}}.

(289)

Now it only remains to find
\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}
satisfying
\Pr(\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)\geq\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}(\alpha,\beta))\leq\beta
. To achieve this we will develop quantile bounds for
\sup_{\pi,\ell}\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}\|_{F}
and
\sup_{\pi,\ell}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell})
.
We begin with
\sup_{\pi,\ell}\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{od}}_{\pi,\ell}\|_{F}
.
Since

\textstyle H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}},\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}};\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{1}}}},\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}})=\langle\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{1}}}}}\mathbf{k},\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}}}\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}

(290)

we have, by the triangle inequality and Cauchy-Schwarz,

\textstyle s_{1}^{2}|a_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi,\ell)|=|H_{\mathrm{ho},m}(\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}},\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}};\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{1}}}},\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}})-\langle\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k},\mu_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}|

(291)

\textstyle=|\langle\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{1}}}}}\mathbf{k},\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}}}\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}-\langle\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k},\mu_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}|

(292)

\textstyle\leq|\langle\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{1}}}}}\mathbf{k}-(\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}),\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}}}\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}|

(293)

\textstyle\quad+|\langle\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k},\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}}}\mathbf{k}-(\mu_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k})\rangle_{\mathbf{k}}|

(294)

\textstyle\leq\|\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{1}}}}}\mathbf{k}-(\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k})\|_{\mathbf{k}}\|\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}}}\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}

(295)

\textstyle\quad+\|\mu_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\|\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}}\mathbf{k}-\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}+\ell_{k_{2}}}}}\mathbf{k}-(\mu_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k}-\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}\mathbf{k})\|_{\mathbf{k}}

(296)

\textstyle\leq(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))

(297)

\textstyle\qquad\times(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mu_{k_{2}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}))

(298)

\textstyle\quad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mu_{k_{1}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}})(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})).

(299)

Hence, by Jensen’s inequality and the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}s_{1}^{4}|a_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi,\ell)|^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\bigg((\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mu_{k_{2}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}))\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mu_{k_{1}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}})(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}))\bigg)^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}3(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}))^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\!+\!3\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mu_{k_{2}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\!+\!3\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mu_{k_{1}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}})(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}))^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\frac{3}{2}\big((\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))^{4}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\!(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}))^{4}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\!+\!6\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mu_{k_{2}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\!+\!6\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mu_{k_{1}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}})(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}))\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}12\big(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\!+\!6\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{2}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{2}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{2}}))\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\big(24s_{1}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})\!+\!12s_{1}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\!+\!\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\big(24s_{1}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q})\!+\!12s_{1}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q})\big)\end{split}

Let
\mathcal{E}
be the event that the inequality of
Lem.

7
with
\delta=\frac{\beta}{3}
holds simultaneously for all
p\in\{1,2,4\}
.
By the union bound, this holds with probability at least
1-\beta
.
Moreover, on
\mathcal{E}
,

\textstyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}s_{1}^{4}|a_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\pi,\ell)|^{2}

\textstyle<24s_{1}(\mathcal{A}_{4}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\beta/3))^{4}

(300)

\textstyle+12s_{1}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\mathcal{A}_{2}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\beta/3))^{2}

(301)

Therefore, on
\mathcal{E}
,

\textstyle\sup_{\pi,\ell}\|\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell}^{\mathrm{od}}\|_{F}

(302)

\textstyle<\sqrt{\frac{24s_{1}(\mathcal{A}_{4}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\frac{\beta}{3}))^{4}+12s_{1}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\mathcal{A}_{2}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\frac{\beta}{3}))^{2}}{s_{1}^{4}}}

(303)

\textstyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{24}(\mathcal{A}_{4}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\frac{\beta}{3}))^{2}}{s_{1}^{3/2}}+\frac{\sqrt{12}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\mathcal{A}_{2}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\frac{\beta}{3}))}{s_{1}^{3/2}}.

(304)

Next, we bound
\sup_{\pi,\ell}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell})
using Jensen’s inequality:

\textstyle\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell})=\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{s_{1}}a_{k_{1},k_{1}}(\pi,\ell)

(305)

\textstyle\leq\frac{1}{s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{s_{1}}(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))^{2}

(306)

\textstyle\qquad\quad+2\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mu_{k_{1}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}})(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))

(307)

\textstyle\leq\frac{1}{s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{s_{1}}2(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))

(308)

\textstyle\qquad\quad+2\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{k_{1}}}},\mu_{k_{1}})\!+\!\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}_{\pi_{s_{1}\!+\!\ell_{k_{1}}}}},\mu^{\prime}_{k_{1}}))

(309)

\textstyle\leq\frac{2}{s_{1}^{2}}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})+\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q})

(310)

\textstyle\qquad\quad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\big(\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}_{i}},\mathbb{P})+\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}_{i}},\mathbb{Q})\big)\big)

(311)

On
\mathcal{E}
we therefore have

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\sup_{\pi,\ell}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathbf{A}_{\pi,\ell})&\textstyle<\frac{2}{s_{1}^{2}}\big((\mathcal{A}_{2}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\beta/3))^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})(\mathcal{A}_{1}(s_{1},s_{2},m)+\mathcal{B}(s_{1},s_{2},m,\beta/3))\big).\end{split}

From

302
,
287
, and
D.6
, we therefore obtain that with probability at least

1-\beta
,

\textstyle\Psi_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)

\textstyle<\Psi_{n_{1},n_{2},s}(\alpha,\beta).

(312)

Appendix E
Proof of

Thm.

2
:
Theorem 2

(Power of cheap independence: finite variance)
.

We will establish both claims using the refined two moments method (
Corollary

1
) with the auxiliary sequence
(V_{n}^{\pi_{b},s})_{b=1}^{B}
of cheaply-permuted independence V-statistics
93
.
By
Corollary

1
with option
47
, it suffices to bound
\mathrm{Var}(V_{n})
,
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,s}\mid\mathbb{X})]
,
\mathrm{Var}(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}\mid\mathbb{X}])
, and
\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}]
.
These quantities are bounded in
Lems.

9
,
10
, and
11
with proofs in
Secs.

E.1
,
E.2
, and
E.3
.

Lemma 9

(Variance of independence V-statistics)
.

Under the assumptions of
Thm.

2
,

\textstyle\mathrm{Var}(V_{n})\leq\frac{16\psi_{1}^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{144\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}.

(313)

Lemma 10

(Conditional variance of permuted independence V-statistics)
.

Under the assumptions of
Thm.

2
,

\textstyle\max(\mathrm{Var}(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,n}\mid\mathbb{X}]),\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,n}\mid\mathbb{X})])\leq\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,n})\leq\frac{32\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{960\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad

(314)

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\max(\mathrm{Var}(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}\mid\mathbb{X}]),\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,s}\mid\mathbb{X})])\leq\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,s})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\leq\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\big(\frac{32}{n}+\frac{2304}{ns}+\frac{460800}{ns^{2}}\big)+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big(\frac{960}{n^{2}}+\frac{4147200}{n^{2}s}\big).\end{split}

Lemma 11

(Mean of permuted independence V-statistics)
.

Under the assumptions of
Thm.

2
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,n}]

\textstyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{U}+\frac{10n^{2}-44n+48}{n^{3}}\tilde{\xi}\quad\text{and}\quad

(315)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}]

\textstyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\big(1+\frac{s^{2}+s-4}{s^{3}}\big)\mathcal{U}+\frac{10n^{2}-44n+48}{n^{3}}\tilde{\xi}.

(316)

Now fix any
\alpha,\beta\in(0,1)
.
By
Lem.

10
,

\textstyle\sqrt{(\frac{2}{\alpha\beta}\!-\frac{2}{\beta})\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,s}\mid\mathbb{X})]}+\!\sqrt{\!(\frac{2}{\beta}-1)\mathrm{Var}(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}\mid\mathbb{X}])}

(317)

\textstyle\leq\big(\sqrt{\frac{2}{\alpha\beta}\!-\!\frac{2}{\beta}}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta}-1}\big)\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,s})}\leq\sqrt{\big(\frac{4}{\alpha\beta}\!-\!2\big)\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,s})},

(318)

with the same result holding when
V_{n}^{\pi,n}
is substituted for
V_{n}^{\pi,s}
.
Hence, by

Corollaries

1
,
9
,
10
, and
11
the standard and cheap tests enjoy power at least
1-\beta
whenever

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]\!-\!\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{U}\geq\sqrt{(\frac{3}{\beta}-1)\big(\frac{16\psi_{1}^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{144\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}+\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\frac{32\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{960\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}+\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi}\text{ and }

(319)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]\!-\!\frac{1}{4}\big(1+\frac{s^{2}+s-4}{s^{3}}\big)\mathcal{U}\geq\sqrt{(\frac{3}{\beta}-1)\big(\frac{16\psi_{1}^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{144\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}

(320)

\textstyle\qquad\ +\!\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\big(\frac{32}{n}+\frac{2304}{ns}+\frac{460800}{ns^{2}}\big)+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big(\frac{960}{n^{2}}+\frac{4147200}{n^{2}s}\big)\big)}\!+\!\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi}

(321)

respectively.
To recover the looser sufficient condition
40
for standard testing, we note that, by
Lem.

12
,
\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]\geq\mathcal{U}-\frac{24\sqrt{\psi_{2}^{\prime}}+6\tilde{\xi}}{n}
.
To recover the looser sufficient condition
39
for cheap testing, we again invoke
\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]\geq\mathcal{U}-\frac{24\sqrt{\psi_{2}^{\prime}}+6\tilde{\xi}}{n}
and additionally note that

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\sqrt{(\frac{3}{\beta}-1)\big(\frac{16\psi_{1}^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{144\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}\!+\!\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\big(\frac{32}{n}+\frac{2304}{ns}+\frac{460800}{ns^{2}}\big)+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big(\frac{960}{n^{2}}+\frac{4147200}{n^{2}s}\big)\big)}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\quad+\!\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi}\!+\!\frac{24\sqrt{\psi_{2}^{\prime}}+6\tilde{\xi}}{n}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\frac{3}{4}\gamma_{n}+\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\big(\frac{2304}{ns}+\frac{460800}{ns^{2}}\big)+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\frac{4147200}{n^{2}s}\big)}.\end{split}

Lemma 12

(Independence V vs. U-statistics)
.

Let
U_{n}
be the U-statistic associated to
h_{\mathrm{in}}
, which takes the form
U_{n}=\frac{1}{(n)_{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathbf{i}_{4}^{n}}h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})
, and satisfies
\mathbb{E}[U_{n}]=\mathbb{E}[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4})]=\mathcal{U}
.
Under the assumptions of
Thm.

2
,

\textstyle|\mathbb{E}[V_{n}-U_{n}]|\leq\frac{24\sqrt{\psi_{2}^{\prime}}+6\tilde{\xi}}{n}.

(322)

Proof.

By definition,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle V_{n}-U_{n}&\textstyle=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in[n]^{4}}h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad-\frac{1}{(n)_{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathbf{i}_{4}^{n}}h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\big(\frac{1}{n^{4}}-\frac{1}{(n)_{4}}\big)\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathbf{i}_{4}^{n}}h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad+\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in[n]^{4}\setminus\mathbf{i}_{4}^{n}}h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}}).\end{split}

Thus, we have that

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle|\mathbb{E}[V_{n}-U_{n}]|&\textstyle\leq\big|\frac{1}{n^{4}}-\frac{1}{(n)_{4}}\big|\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathbf{i}_{4}^{n}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})\big]\big|\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad+\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in[n]^{4}\setminus\mathbf{i}_{4}^{n}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})\big]\big|.\end{split}

We bound the first term on the right-hand side
using Jensen’s inequality and the
\psi_{2}^{\prime}
definition:

\textstyle\big|\frac{1}{n^{4}}-\frac{1}{(n)_{4}}\big|\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathbf{i}_{4}^{n}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})\big]\big|

(323)

\textstyle\leq\big|\frac{1}{n^{4}}-\frac{1}{(n)_{4}}\big|\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathbf{i}_{4}^{n}}\big(\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})^{2}\big]\big)^{1/2}

(324)

\textstyle\leq\big|\frac{1}{n^{4}}-\frac{1}{(n)_{4}}\big|(n)_{4}\big(16\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big)^{1/2}.

(325)

We bound the second term using the definition of
\tilde{\xi}
:

\textstyle\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in[n]^{4}\setminus\mathbf{i}_{4}^{n}}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})\big]\big|\leq\big|\frac{1}{n^{4}}-\frac{1}{(n)_{4}}\big|(n)_{4}\tilde{\xi}.

(326)

The advertised conclusion follows as
1-\frac{(n)_{4}}{n^{4}}=1-\frac{(n-1)(n-2)(n-3)}{n^{3}}=\frac{6n^{2}-11n+6}{n^{3}}\leq\frac{6}{n}
.
∎

E.1
Proof of

Lem.

9
:
Lemma 9

(Variance of independence V-statistics)
.

Define the index sets

\textstyle\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}

\textstyle\triangleq[n]^{8},

(327)

\textstyle\mathsf{K}_{1}

\textstyle\triangleq\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4},i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}^{\prime},i_{4}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:|\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}\}\cap\{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}^{\prime},i_{4}^{\prime}\}|=0\},

(328)

\textstyle\mathsf{K}_{2}

\textstyle\triangleq\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4},i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}^{\prime},i_{4}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:|\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}\}\cap\{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}^{\prime},i_{4}^{\prime}\}|=1\},\text{ and }

(329)

\textstyle(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}

\textstyle\triangleq\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4},i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}^{\prime},i_{4}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:|\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}\}\cap\{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}^{\prime},i_{4}^{\prime}\}|>1\}.

(330)

Then,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\mathrm{Var}(V_{n})&\textstyle=\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\frac{1}{n^{8}}\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{4},i_{1}^{\prime},\dots,i_{4}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}}\big(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}^{\prime}},X_{i_{2}^{\prime}},X_{i_{3}^{\prime}},X_{i_{4}^{\prime}})]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}^{\prime}},X_{i_{2}^{\prime}},X_{i_{3}^{\prime}},X_{i_{4}^{\prime}})]\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=(I)+(II)+(III),\end{split}

where
(I)
is the summation over
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{K}_{1}
,
(II)
is the summation over
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{K}_{2}
, and
(III)
is the summation over
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4}^{\prime})\in(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}
. Note that

\textstyle|\mathsf{K}_{2}|\leq n^{4}\cdot 4\cdot 4\cdot n^{3}=16n^{7}\quad\text{and}\quad|(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}|\leq n^{4}\cdot 4\cdot 3\cdot 4\cdot 3\cdot n^{2}=144n^{6}.

(331)

We also remark that for
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{K}_{1}
,
\mathbb{E}[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}^{\prime}},X_{i_{2}^{\prime}},X_{i_{3}^{\prime}},X_{i_{4}^{\prime}})]=\mathbb{E}[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\mathbb{E}[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}^{\prime}},X_{i_{2}^{\prime}},X_{i_{3}^{\prime}},X_{i_{4}^{\prime}})]
,
which implies that
(I)=0
.

By the definition
Sec.

4.2
of
\psi_{1}^{\prime}
, we have that
(II)\leq\frac{1}{n^{8}}|\mathsf{K}_{2}|\psi_{1}^{\prime}\leq\frac{16\psi_{1}^{\prime}}{n}
.
By the nonnegativity of
\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}
, Cauchy-Schwarz, Jensen’s inequality, and the definition
Sec.

4.2
of
\psi_{2}^{\prime}
, we have that each summand of
(III)
is upper-bounded by

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}^{\prime}},X_{i_{2}^{\prime}},X_{i_{3}^{\prime}},X_{i_{4}^{\prime}})]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\big(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})^{2}]\big)^{1/2}\big(\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}^{\prime}},X_{i_{2}^{\prime}},X_{i_{3}^{\prime}},X_{i_{4}^{\prime}})^{2}]\big)^{1/2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\big(\mathbb{E}[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})^{2}]\big)^{1/2}\big(\mathbb{E}[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}^{\prime}},X_{i_{2}^{\prime}},X_{i_{3}^{\prime}},X_{i_{4}^{\prime}})^{2}]\big)^{1/2}\leq\psi_{2}^{\prime}.\end{split}

Hence,
(III)\leq\frac{1}{n^{8}}|(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}|\psi_{2}^{\prime}\leq\frac{144\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}
, which concludes the proof.

E.2
Proof of

Lem.

10
:
Lemma 10

(Conditional variance of permuted independence V-statistics)
.

Throughout we use the shorthand

\mathfrak{i}\triangleq(i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2},i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime})
.

E.2.1
Proof of
314

Our approach is similar to that employed in
[
31
, Thm. 5.1]
to study independence U-statistics.
We begin by defining the convenient shorthand

\textstyle\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})

\textstyle\triangleq|\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4},n\!+\!1\!-\!i_{1},n\!+\!1\!-\!i_{2},n\!+\!1\!-\!i_{3},n\!+\!1\!-\!i_{4}\}\cap\{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}^{\prime},i_{4}^{\prime}\}|,

(332)

\textstyle\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}

\textstyle\triangleq[n]^{8},\quad\mathsf{K}_{1}\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})=0\},\quad\mathsf{K}_{2}\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})=1\},\quad\text{and}\quad

(333)

\textstyle(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}

\textstyle\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})>1\},

(334)

and writing

\textstyle h_{\mathrm{in}}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})

\textstyle=h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}),\quad\text{for}\quad

(335)

\textstyle h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})

\textstyle\triangleq g_{Y}(y_{1},y_{2})+g_{Y}(y_{3},y_{4})-g_{Y}(y_{1},y_{3})-g_{Y}(y_{2},y_{4})\quad\text{and}\quad

(336)

\textstyle h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4})

\textstyle\triangleq g_{Z}(z_{1},z_{2})+g_{Z}(z_{3},z_{4})-g_{Z}(z_{1},z_{3})-g_{Z}(z_{2},z_{4}).

(337)

By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,n}|\mathbb{X})]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[(V_{n}^{\pi,n})^{2}|\mathbb{X}]-(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,n}|\mathbb{X}])^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[(V_{n}^{\pi,n})^{2}]-(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,n}])^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle~=~\frac{1}{n^{8}}\sum_{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathfrak{H}_{Y}\mathfrak{H}^{\prime}_{Y}h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}^{\prime}}})\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathfrak{H}_{Y}h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\mathfrak{H}^{\prime}_{Y}h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}^{\prime}}})\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle~=~(I)+(II)+(III),\end{split}

where
\mathfrak{H}_{Y}\triangleq h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})
,
\mathfrak{H}^{\prime}_{Y}\triangleq h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i^{\prime}_{1}},Y_{i^{\prime}_{2}},Y_{i^{\prime}_{3}},Y_{i^{\prime}_{4}})
, and
(I),(II),(III)
are the summations over
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{2}
, and
\mathfrak{i}\in(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}
respectively.

Note that for
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{1}
,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathfrak{H}_{Y}\mathfrak{H}_{Y}^{\prime}h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}^{\prime}}})\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathfrak{H}_{Y}\mathfrak{H}_{Y}^{\prime}h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}^{\prime}}})\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathfrak{H}_{Y}h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\mathfrak{H}_{Y}^{\prime}h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}^{\prime}}})\big],\end{split}

so that
(I)=0
.
Also, note that

\textstyle|\mathsf{K}_{2}|\leq n^{4}\cdot 2\cdot 4\cdot 4\cdot n^{3}=32n^{7}\quad\text{and}\quad|(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}|\leq n^{4}\cdot 2\cdot 4\cdot 3\cdot 2\cdot 4\cdot 3\cdot n^{2}=576n^{6}.

(338)

For
i,j\in[4]
, let us define
\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2,i,j}
as the set of indices
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{2}
with
|\mathfrak{i}|=7
and either
i_{i}=i^{\prime}_{j}
or
i_{i}=n\!+\!1\!-\!i^{\prime}_{j}
.
We further define
\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}
as the union of these 16 sets.
Note that
\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2}
can be characterized as the set of
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{2}
such that, for exactly three indices
i,j,k
of the eight indices, the pairs
\{i,n\!+\!1\!-\!i\}
,
\{j,n\!+\!1\!-\!j\}
and
\{k,n\!+\!1\!-\!k\}
are equal. This characterization allows us to write

\textstyle|\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|=4\cdot 4\cdot 6\cdot 2^{2}\cdot n^{6}=384n^{6}.

(339)

Note that if
\mathfrak{i}\in\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}
with
i_{1}=i^{\prime}_{1}
, then the corresponding summand from
Sec.

E.2.1
equals

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{4})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{1}},Z_{\pi_{2}},Z_{\pi_{3}},Z_{\pi_{4}})

(340)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{4})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{1}},Z_{\pi_{2}},Z_{\pi_{3}},Z_{\pi_{4}})\big]\big)

(341)

\textstyle\qquad\times\big(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{5},Y_{6},Y_{7})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{1}},Z_{\pi_{5}},Z_{\pi_{6}},Z_{\pi_{7}})

(342)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{5},Y_{6},Y_{7})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{1}},Z_{\pi_{5}},Z_{\pi_{6}},Z_{\pi_{7}})\big]\big)\big]

(343)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{Var}(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{4})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{1}},Z_{\pi_{2}},Z_{\pi_{3}},Z_{\pi_{4}})\mid Y_{1},\pi_{1},Z_{\pi_{1}})\big]=\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1},

(344)

where the last equality holds since the distribution of
\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}((Y_{1},Z_{\pi_{1}}),(Y_{2},Z_{\pi_{2}}),(Y_{3},Z_{\pi_{3}}),(Y_{4},Z_{\pi_{4}}))
conditional on
(Y_{1},Z_{\pi_{1}},\pi_{1})
is invariant to the order of the four arguments of
\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}
and hence

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[h_{\mathrm{in}}((Y_{1},Z_{\pi_{1}}),(Y_{2},Z_{\pi_{2}}),(Y_{3},Z_{\pi_{3}}),(Y_{4},Z_{\pi_{4}}))\mid(Y_{1},Z_{\pi_{1}},\pi_{1})]

(345)

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}_{\mathrm{in}}((Y_{1},Z_{\pi_{1}}),(Y_{2},Z_{\pi_{2}}),(Y_{3},Z_{\pi_{3}}),(Y_{4},Z_{\pi_{4}}))\mid(Y_{1},Z_{\pi_{1}},\pi_{1})].

(346)

Similarly, if
\mathfrak{i}\in\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}
with
i_{1}=n+1-i^{\prime}_{1}
,
then the corresponding summand of
Sec.

E.2.1
equals

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{4})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{1}},Z_{\pi_{2}},Z_{\pi_{3}},Z_{\pi_{4}})

(347)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{4})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{1}},Z_{\pi_{2}},Z_{\pi_{3}},Z_{\pi_{4}})\big]\big)

(348)

\textstyle\qquad\times\big(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{n},Y_{5},Y_{6},Y_{7})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{n}},Z_{\pi_{5}},Z_{\pi_{6}},Z_{\pi_{7}})

(349)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{n},Y_{5},Y_{6},Y_{7})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{n}},Z_{\pi_{5}},Z_{\pi_{6}},Z_{\pi_{7}})\big]\big)\big]

(350)

\textstyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{4})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{1}},Z_{\pi_{2}},Z_{\pi_{3}},Z_{\pi_{4}})\mid Y_{1},\pi_{1},Z_{\pi_{1}})]

(351)

\textstyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{n},Y_{5},Y_{6},Y_{7})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{n}},Z_{\pi_{5}},Z_{\pi_{6}},Z_{\pi_{7}})\mid Y_{n},\pi_{n},Z_{\pi_{n}})]=\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1},

(352)

by Cauchy-Schwarz and the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality.

Moreover, for any
\mathfrak{i}\in(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}\cup(\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2})
, the corresponding summand of
Sec.

E.2.1
equals

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})

(353)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})\big]\big)

(354)

\textstyle\qquad\times\big(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}^{\prime}},Y_{i_{2}^{\prime}},Y_{i_{3}^{\prime}},Y_{i_{4}^{\prime}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}^{\prime}}})

(355)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}^{\prime}},Y_{i_{2}^{\prime}},Y_{i_{3}^{\prime}},Y_{i_{4}^{\prime}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}^{\prime}}})\big]\big)\big]

(356)

\textstyle\leq\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\big(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})\big)^{2}\big]\big)^{1/2}

(357)

\textstyle\qquad\times\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\big(h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}^{\prime}},Y_{i_{2}^{\prime}},Y_{i_{3}^{\prime}},Y_{i_{4}^{\prime}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}^{\prime}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}^{\prime}}})\big)^{2}\big]\big)^{1/2}\leq\psi_{2}^{\prime}

(358)

by Cauchy-Schwarz.
Thus,
(II)
is upper-bounded by
\psi_{2}^{\prime}\frac{|\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|}{n^{8}}+\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\frac{|\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|}{n^{8}}
, and we find that

\textstyle\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,n})

\textstyle\leq\psi_{2}^{\prime}\frac{|(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}|+|\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|}{n^{8}}+\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\frac{|\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|}{n^{8}}\leq\frac{960\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}+\frac{32\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}}{n}.

(359)

Finally, by Jensen’s inequality,

\textstyle\mathrm{Var}(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,n}|\mathbb{X}])

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}[(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,n}|\mathbb{X}])^{2}]-(\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,n}|\mathbb{X}]])^{2}

(360)

\textstyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[(V_{n}^{\pi,n})^{2}|\mathbb{X}]]-(\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,n}|\mathbb{X}]])^{2}=\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,n}).

(361)

E.2.2
Proof of
314

Our argument is analogous to that of
Sec.

E.2.1
with
s
substituted for
n
and
H_{\mathrm{in},m}
substituted for
h_{\mathrm{in}}
.
We begin by defining the convenient shorthand

\textstyle\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})

\textstyle\triangleq|\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4},s\!+\!1\!-\!i_{1},s\!+\!1\!-\!i_{2},s\!+\!1\!-\!i_{3},s\!+\!1\!-\!i_{4}\}\cap\{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}^{\prime},i_{4}^{\prime}\}|,

(362)

\textstyle\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}

\textstyle\triangleq[s]^{8},\quad\mathsf{K}_{1}\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})=0\},\quad\mathsf{K}_{2}\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})=1\},\quad\text{and}\quad

(363)

\textstyle(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}

\textstyle\triangleq\{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{i})>1\},

(364)

and define
\bar{K}_{2}
as the set of indices
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{2}
with
|\mathfrak{i}|=7
and either
i_{i}=i^{\prime}_{j}
or
i_{i}=n\!+\!1\!-\!i^{\prime}_{j}
for some
i,j\in[4]
.
As in
Sec.

E.2.1
case we have

\textstyle|\mathsf{K}_{2}|\leq 32s^{7},\qquad|(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}|\leq 576s^{6},\qquad|\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|=384s^{6}.

(365)

Next, we define

\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2}
as the set of
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{2}
such that for exactly three indices
i,j,k
of the eight indices, the pairs
\{i,s\!+\!1\!-\!i\}
,
\{j,s\!+\!1\!-\!j\},
and
\{k,s\!+\!1\!-\!k\}
are equal, and the rest of such pairs are all different. We then have

\textstyle|\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|

\textstyle=4\cdot 4\cdot 6\cdot 2^{2}\cdot s(s-2)(s-4)(s-6)(s-8)(s-10)

(366)

\textstyle=384s(s-2)(s-4)(s-6)(s-8)(s-10)\quad\text{and}\quad

(367)

\textstyle|\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus(\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}\cup\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2})|

\textstyle=|\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|-|\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|

(368)

\textstyle\leq 384(s^{6}-s(s-2)(s-4)(s-6)(s-8)(s-10))\leq 384\cdot 5\cdot 6s^{5}=11520s^{5}.

(369)

We next define
\mathsf{K}_{3}
as the set of
\mathfrak{i}\in(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}
such that for exactly four indices
i,j,k,r
of the eight indices, we have that
\{i,s\!+\!1\!-\!i\}=\{j,s\!+\!1\!-\!j\}
and
\{k,s\!+\!1\!-\!k\}=\{r,s\!+\!1\!-\!r\}
, and the rest of such pairs are all distinct. Note that

\textstyle|\mathsf{K}_{3}|=4\cdot 3\cdot 2\cdot 4\cdot 3\cdot 2s(s-2)(s-4)(s-6)(s-8)(s-10)

(370)

\textstyle\quad=576s(s-2)(s-4)(s-6)(s-8)(s-10)\quad\text{and}\quad

(371)

\textstyle|(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2}\cup\mathsf{K}_{3})^{c}|=|(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}|-|\mathsf{K}_{3}|

(372)

\textstyle\quad\leq 576(s^{6}-s(s-2)(s-4)(s-6)(s-8)(s-10))\leq 576\cdot 5\cdot 6\cdot s^{5}=17280s^{5}.

(373)

Finally, for
\mathfrak{K}\triangleq(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4},k^{\prime}_{1},k^{\prime}_{2},k^{\prime}_{3},k^{\prime}_{4})
and
\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})\triangleq|\{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\}\cap\{k_{1}^{\prime},k_{2}^{\prime},k_{3}^{\prime},k_{4}^{\prime}\}|
, we define the index sets

\textstyle\mathsf{L}_{\text{total}}

\textstyle=[m]^{8},\quad\mathsf{L}_{1}=\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{L}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})=0\},\quad\mathsf{L}_{2}=\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{L}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})=1\},\quad\text{and}\quad

(374)

\textstyle(\mathsf{L}_{1}\cup\mathsf{L}_{2})^{c}

\textstyle=\{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{L}_{\text{total}}:\mathfrak{s}(\mathfrak{K})>1\}

(375)

and note that

\textstyle|\mathsf{L}_{2}|

\textstyle\leq m^{4}\cdot 4\cdot 4\cdot m^{3}=16m^{7}\quad\text{and}\quad|(\mathsf{L}_{1}\cup\mathsf{L}_{2})^{c}|\leq m^{4}\cdot 4\cdot 3\cdot 4\cdot 3\cdot m^{2}=144m^{6}.

(376)

With this notation in hand, we use Cauchy-Schwarz to write

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X})]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[(V_{n}^{\pi,s})^{2}|\mathbb{X}]-(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X}])^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[(V_{n}^{\pi,s})^{2}]-(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}])^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle~=~\frac{1}{n^{8}}\sum_{\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{\text{total}}}\sum_{\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{L}_{\text{total}}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{k_{1}}^{(i_{1})},Y_{k_{2}}^{(i_{2})},Y_{k_{3}}^{(i_{3})},Y_{k_{4}}^{(i_{4})})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{k^{\prime}_{1}}^{(i^{\prime}_{1})},Y_{k^{\prime}_{2}}^{(i^{\prime}_{2})},Y_{k^{\prime}_{3}}^{(i^{\prime}_{3})},Y_{k^{\prime}_{4}}^{(i^{\prime}_{4})})\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\times h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{k_{1}}^{(\pi_{i_{1}})},Z_{k_{2}}^{(\pi_{i_{2}})},Z_{k_{3}}^{(\pi_{i_{3}})},Z_{k_{4}}^{(\pi_{i_{4}})})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{k^{\prime}_{1}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{1}})},Z_{k^{\prime}_{2}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{2}})},Z_{k^{\prime}_{3}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{3}})},Z_{k^{\prime}_{4}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{4}})})\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{k_{1}}^{(i_{1})},Y_{k_{2}}^{(i_{2})},Y_{k_{3}}^{(i_{3})},Y_{k_{4}}^{(i_{4})})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{k_{1}}^{(\pi_{i_{1}})},Z_{k_{2}}^{(\pi_{i_{2}})},Z_{k_{3}}^{(\pi_{i_{3}})},Z_{k_{4}}^{(\pi_{i_{4}})})\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{k^{\prime}_{1}}^{(i^{\prime}_{1})},Y_{k^{\prime}_{2}}^{(i^{\prime}_{2})},Y_{k^{\prime}_{3}}^{(i^{\prime}_{3})},Y_{k^{\prime}_{4}}^{(i^{\prime}_{4})})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{k^{\prime}_{1}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{1}})},Z_{k^{\prime}_{2}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{2}})},Z_{k^{\prime}_{3}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{3}})},Z_{k^{\prime}_{4}}^{(\pi_{i^{\prime}_{4}})})\big]\bigg)\\[5.0pt]
\textstyle&\textstyle~=~(I^{\prime})+(II^{\prime})+(III^{\prime}),\end{split}

where
(I^{\prime})
is the summation over
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{1}
,
(II^{\prime})
is the summation over
\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{2}
, and
(III^{\prime})
is the summation over
\mathfrak{i}\in(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2})^{c}
. Note that
(I^{\prime})=0
by the same argument that we used to show that
(I)=0
in (
E.2.1
).

To bound
(II^{\prime})
, we will further divide the corresponding summands into subcases:

•

\mathfrak{i}\in\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}
: Suppose without loss of generality that
i_{1}=i^{\prime}_{1}
. The only non-zero summands in the sum over
\mathfrak{K}
have
k_{1}=k^{\prime}_{1}
. There are
m^{7}
such summands, and each contributes at most
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}
to the sum.

•

\mathfrak{i}\in\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2}
:
Consider the three indices of
\mathfrak{i}
that satisfy the defining
\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2}
property. In the summation over
\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{L}_{\text{total}}
, a summand contributes at most
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}
when exactly two of the three
\mathfrak{i}
indices share the same
k
-index, and there are at most
3m^{7}
such summands. The only other non-zero summands contribute at most
\psi_{2}^{\prime}
when all three
\mathfrak{i}
indices share the same
k
-index, and there are are most
m^{6}
such summands.

•

\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus(\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}\cup\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2})
: Considering the summation over
\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{L}_{\text{total}}
, there will be a contribution bounded by
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}
when
\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{L}_{2}
, and there are at most
16m^{7}
such summands, The only other non-zero contributions are bounded by
\psi_{2}^{\prime}
and occur when
\mathfrak{K}\in(\mathsf{L}_{1}\cup\mathsf{L}_{2})^{c}
, so the number of such summands is bounded by
144m^{6}
.

Thus, we have that
(II^{\prime})
is upper-bounded by

\textstyle\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}\frac{|\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|m^{7}}{n^{8}}+\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}\frac{|\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|\cdot 3m^{7}}{n^{8}}+\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}\frac{|\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus(\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}\cup\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2})|\cdot 16m^{7}}{n^{8}}+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\frac{|\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2}|\cdot m^{6}}{n^{8}}+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\frac{|\mathsf{K}_{2}\setminus(\bar{\mathsf{K}}_{2}\cup\tilde{\mathsf{K}}_{2})|\cdot|(\mathsf{L}_{1}\cup\mathsf{L}_{2})^{c}|}{n^{8}}

(377)

\textstyle\leq\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\big(\frac{32s^{7}m^{7}}{n^{8}}+\frac{384s^{6}\cdot 3m^{7}}{n^{8}}+\frac{11520s^{5}\cdot 16m^{7}}{n^{8}}\big)+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big(\frac{384s^{6}m^{6}}{n^{8}}+\frac{11520s^{5}\cdot 144m^{6}}{n^{8}}\big).

(378)

Similarly, to bound
(III^{\prime})
, we divide the corresponding summands into subcases:

•

\mathfrak{i}\in\mathsf{K}_{3}
:
Consider the two pairs of indices of
\mathfrak{i}
satisfying the defining property of
\mathsf{K}_{3}
.
When each pair respectively shares the same
k
-index, the summand contributes at most
\psi^{\prime}_{2}
, and there are at most
m^{6}
such summands.
The only other non-zero summands have exactly one of the two pairs sharing a
k
-index; these summands contribute at most
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}
, and there are at most
2m^{7}
such summands.

•

\mathfrak{i}\in(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2}\cup\mathsf{K}_{3})^{c}
: In the summation over
\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{L}_{\text{total}}
, a summand will contribute at most
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}
when
\mathfrak{K}\in\mathsf{L}_{2}
, and there are at most
16m^{7}
such summands. The only other non-zero summands are bounded by
\psi^{\prime}_{2}
and occur when
\mathfrak{K}\in(\mathsf{L}_{1}\cup\mathsf{L}_{2})^{c}
, so there are at most
144m^{6}
such summands.

Thus,
(III^{\prime})
is upper-bounded by

\textstyle\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}\frac{|\mathsf{K}_{3}|\cdot 2m^{7}}{n^{8}}+\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}\frac{|(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2}\cup\mathsf{K}_{3})^{c}|\cdot|\mathsf{L}_{2}|}{n^{8}}+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\frac{|\mathsf{K}_{3}|\cdot m^{6}}{n^{8}}+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\frac{|(\mathsf{K}_{1}\cup\mathsf{K}_{2}\cup\mathsf{K}_{3})^{c}|\cdot|(\mathsf{L}_{1}\cup\mathsf{L}_{2})^{c}|}{n^{8}}

(379)

\textstyle\leq\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}\big(\frac{576s^{6}\cdot 2m^{7}}{n^{8}}+\frac{17280s^{5}\cdot 16m^{7}}{n^{8}}\big)+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big(\frac{576s^{6}\cdot m^{6}}{n^{8}}+\frac{17280s^{5}\cdot 144m^{6}}{n^{8}}\big)

(380)

We conclude that

\textstyle\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,s})

\textstyle\leq\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\big(\frac{32}{n}+\frac{2304}{ns}+\frac{460800}{ns^{2}}\big)+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big(\frac{960}{n^{2}}+\frac{4147200}{n^{2}s}\big).

(381)

Finally, by Jensen’s inequality,

\textstyle\mathrm{Var}(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X}])

\textstyle=\mathbb{E}[(\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X}])^{2}]-(\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X}]])^{2}

(382)

\textstyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[(V_{n}^{\pi,s})^{2}|\mathbb{X}]]-(\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}|\mathbb{X}]])^{2}=\mathrm{Var}(V_{n}^{\pi,s}).

(383)

E.3
Proof of

Lem.

11
:
Lemma 11

(Mean of permuted independence V-statistics)
.

E.3.1
Proof of
315

Define the sets of indices

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}&\textstyle\triangleq[n]^{4},\\
\textstyle\mathsf{J}_{1}&\textstyle\triangleq\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}:\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad|\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4},\texttt{swap}(i_{1}),\texttt{swap}(i_{2}),\texttt{swap}(i_{3}),\texttt{swap}(i_{4})\}|=8\},\\
\textstyle\mathsf{J}_{2}&\textstyle\triangleq\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathsf{J}_{\text{total}}:\ (i_{1}=i_{4})\vee(i_{2}=i_{3})\},\end{split}

and note that

|\mathsf{J}_{1}|=n(n-2)(n-4)(n-6)
,

|\mathsf{J}_{2}|=2n^{3}
, and

\textstyle|(\mathsf{J}_{1}\cup\mathsf{J}_{2})^{c}|=n^{4}-(n^{4}-12n^{3}+44n^{2}-48n)-2n^{3}=10n^{3}-44n^{2}+48n.

(384)

Recalling the definitions (
84
) of
h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}
and
h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}
, we have

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,n}]

\textstyle=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{4})\in[n]^{4}}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})\big]

(385)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{4})\in[n]^{4}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})\mid\pi\big]\big]

(386)

\textstyle\leq\frac{n(n-2)(n-4)(n-6)}{n^{4}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]+\frac{10n^{3}-44n^{2}+48n}{n^{4}}\tilde{\xi}\leq\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]+\frac{10n^{2}-44n+48}{n^{3}}\tilde{\xi}\quad\text{for}\quad

(387)

\textstyle\mathcal{U}_{\pi}

\textstyle\triangleq\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3},Y_{4})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{1}},Z_{\pi_{2}},Z_{\pi_{3}},Z_{\pi_{4}})\mid\pi\big].

(388)

The penultimate inequality follows from the following facts:

•

For
(i_{1},\dots,i_{4})\in\mathsf{J}_{1}
,
\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})|\pi\big]=\mathcal{U}_{\pi}
.

•

For
(i_{1},\dots,i_{4})\in\mathsf{J}_{2}
,
\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})|\pi\big]=0
.

•

For
(i_{1},\dots,i_{4})\in(\mathsf{J}_{1}\cup\mathsf{J}_{2})^{c}
,
\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})|\pi\big]\leq\tilde{\xi}
, where
\tilde{\xi}
is defined in (
37
).

Finally, equation
392
of the following lemma shows that
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}]
completing the proof.

Lemma 13

(Range and expectation of
\mathcal{U}_{\pi}
)
.

Instantiate the assumptions of
Thm.

2
, fix
any
i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}\in[n]
satisfying

\textstyle|\{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4},\texttt{swap}(i_{1}),\texttt{swap}(i_{2}),\texttt{swap}(i_{3}),\texttt{swap}(i_{4})\}|=8,

(389)

and let
\pi
be any permutation on
[n]
with

\textstyle(\pi_{i_{j}},\pi_{\texttt{swap}(i_{j})})\sim\textnormal{Unif}(\{(i_{j},\texttt{swap}(i_{j})),(\texttt{swap}(i_{j}),i_{j})\})\quad\text{for each}\quad j\in[4].

(390)

Then we always have

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\mathcal{U}_{\pi}&\textstyle\triangleq\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}},Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{i_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{i_{4}}})|\pi\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\in\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\{0,\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2},1\}\end{split}

with

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]

\textstyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})].

(391)

If (
\pi_{i_{1}}
,
\pi_{i_{2}}
,
\pi_{i_{3}}
,
\pi_{i_{4}}
) are independent, then

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]

\textstyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})].

(392)

If
\pi_{i_{1}}=i_{1}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{4}}=i_{4}

with independent
(\pi_{i_{1}},\pi_{i_{2}},\pi_{i_{3}})
or
\pi_{i_{2}}=i_{2}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{3}}=i_{3}

with independent
(\pi_{i_{1}},\pi_{i_{2}},\pi_{i_{4}})
, then

\textstyle\ \mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]

\textstyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})].

(393)

If
\pi_{i_{1}}=i_{1}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{2}}=i_{2}
with independent
(\pi_{i_{1}},\pi_{i_{3}},\pi_{i_{4}})
;
\pi_{i_{1}}=i_{1}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{3}}=i_{3}
with independent
(\pi_{i_{1}},\pi_{i_{2}},\pi_{i_{4}})
;
\pi_{i_{2}}=i_{2}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{4}}=i_{4}
with independent
(\pi_{i_{1}},\pi_{i_{2}},\pi_{i_{3}})
; or
\pi_{i_{3}}=i_{3}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{4}}=i_{4}
with independent
(\pi_{i_{1}},\pi_{i_{2}},\pi_{i_{3}})
, then

\textstyle\ \mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]

\textstyle=\frac{3}{8}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})].

(394)

Proof.

We will use the shorthand

\textstyle P_{i,j}\triangleq\begin{cases}\Pi&\text{if }i=j\\
\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(395)

and the notation
(\mathbb{P}^{(i)},\mathbb{Q}^{(i)})
to denote copies of
(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})
while keeping track of indices to make the derivation easier to follow.
We begin by proving
Lem.

13
.

\mathcal{U}_{\pi}
may be rewritten as

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\mathcal{U}_{\pi}&\textstyle=\iint g_{Y}(y,y^{\prime})g_{Z}(z,z^{\prime})\,d(P_{i_{1},\pi_{i_{1}}}+P_{i_{4},\pi_{i_{4}}}-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}^{(\pi_{i_{4}})}-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}^{(\pi_{i_{1}})})(y,z)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad d(P_{i_{2},\pi_{i_{2}}}+P_{i_{3},\pi_{i_{3}}}-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}^{(\pi_{i_{3}})}-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}^{(\pi_{i_{2}})})(y^{\prime},z^{\prime}).\end{split}

Thus, if we define the
\textnormal{Unif}(\{0,1\})
random variables
\epsilon_{i_{k}}=\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{i_{k}}=i_{k}}
, we can write

\textstyle\mathcal{U}_{\pi}

\textstyle=\iint g_{Y}(y,y^{\prime})g_{Z}(z,z^{\prime})\,d(\epsilon_{i_{1}}(\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})+\epsilon_{i_{4}}(\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}))(y,z)

(396)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad d(\epsilon_{i_{2}}(\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})+\epsilon_{i_{3}}(\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}))(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})

(397)

\textstyle=\big(\epsilon_{i_{1}}+\epsilon_{i_{4}}\big)\big(\epsilon_{i_{2}}+\epsilon_{i_{3}}\big)\iint g_{Y}(y,y^{\prime})g_{Z}(z,z^{\prime})\,d(\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})(y,z)

(398)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad d(\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})

(399)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{4}\big(\epsilon_{i_{1}}+\epsilon_{i_{4}}\big)\big(\epsilon_{i_{2}}+\epsilon_{i_{3}}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4})].

(400)

Setting
\epsilon_{i_{1}},\epsilon_{i_{2}},\epsilon_{i_{3}},\epsilon_{i_{4}}
to
\{0,1\}
implies that all the possible values
\mathcal{U}_{\pi}
can take are
\{0,\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2},1\}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4})]
.

To show (
391
), we will prove that for any joint distribution over the
\textnormal{Unif}(\{0,1\})
variables
\epsilon_{i_{1}},\epsilon_{i_{2}},\epsilon_{i_{3}},\epsilon_{i_{4}}
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\big(\epsilon_{i_{1}}+\epsilon_{i_{4}}\big)\big(\epsilon_{i_{2}}+\epsilon_{i_{3}}\big)]\leq 2
. Indeed, by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\epsilon_{i_{1}}+\epsilon_{i_{4}}\big)\big(\epsilon_{i_{2}}+\epsilon_{i_{3}}\big)\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\epsilon_{i_{1}}+\epsilon_{i_{4}}\big)^{2}\big]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\epsilon_{i_{2}}+\epsilon_{i_{3}}\big)^{2}\big]^{1/2},

(401)

and
\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\epsilon_{i_{1}}+\epsilon_{i_{4}}\big)^{2}\big]
is maximized when
\epsilon_{i_{1}}=\epsilon_{i_{4}}
almost surely, with maximum value
0\times\frac{1}{2}+4\times\frac{1}{2}=2
. Thus,
\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\epsilon_{i_{1}}+\epsilon_{i_{4}}\big)\big(\epsilon_{i_{2}}+\epsilon_{i_{3}}\big)\big]
has maximum value 2.

To show
392
, we compute the probability that
\mathcal{U}_{\pi}
takes each of its possible values when
(\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{4})
are independent:

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]=\frac{1}{2^{4}}\big(0\times{4\choose 0}+0\times(2+{4\choose 1})+\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\times 4

(402)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\times{4\choose 3}+\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\times{4\choose 4}\big)

(403)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{16}\big(\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]+2\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]+\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\big)

(404)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})].

(405)

Similarly, when
\pi_{i_{1}}=i_{1}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{4}}=i_{4}
and
(\pi_{i_{1}},\pi_{i_{2}},\pi_{i_{3}})
are independent,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]

\textstyle=\frac{1}{2^{3}}\big(0\times 4+0\times 1+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\times 2+\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\times 1\big)

(406)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})].

(407)

Alternatively, when

\pi_{i_{1}}=i_{1}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{2}}=i_{2}
and
(\pi_{i_{1}},\pi_{i_{3}},\pi_{i_{4}})
are independent,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{U}_{\pi}]

\textstyle=\frac{1}{2^{3}}\big(0\times 3+\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\times 2+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\times 2

(408)

\textstyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})]\times 1\big)=\frac{3}{8}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},X_{i_{3}},X_{i_{4}})].

(409)

∎

E.3.2
Proof of
316

In the cheap permutation case, the permutation values
\pi_{i}
,
\pi_{j}
are not independent when
i
and
j
belong to the same bin, which requires a finer analysis. In the following, for
1\leq i\leq s
, we let
\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)\triangleq i+\frac{s}{2}-s\mathbf{1}_{i>\frac{s}{2}}
and recall that
\texttt{swap}(i)\triangleq i+\frac{n}{2}-n\mathbf{1}_{i>\frac{n}{2}}
.
We have that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}]

(410)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{4})\in[s]^{4}}\sum_{(j_{1},\dots,j_{4})\in\Pi_{k=1}^{4}\mathcal{I}_{i_{k}}}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}},Y_{j_{3}},Y_{j_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{j_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{4}}})\big]

(411)

\textstyle=(I)+(II)+(III)+(IV)+(V),

(412)

where

•

(I)
includes the terms for which the bin indices

i_{1},\dots,i_{4},\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{1}),\dots,\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{4})

are distinct. For such terms,
\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}},Y_{j_{3}},Y_{j_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{j_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{4}}})\big]=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4})]=\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{U}
by equation (
392
) of
Lem.

13
. The number of terms in
(I)
is
s(s-2)(s-4)(s-6)n^{4}
.

•

(II)
includes the terms
for which there are exactly two pairs of repeated bin indices among
i_{1},\dots,i_{4},\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{1}),\dots,\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{4})
, and the two pairs are one of the following:
\{(i_{1},i_{4}),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{1}),\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{4}))\}
,
\{(i_{1},\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{4})),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{1}),i_{4})\}
(so that
\pi_{i_{1}}=i_{1}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{4}}=i_{4}
), or
\{(i_{2},i_{3}),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{2}),\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{3}))\}
,
\{(i_{2},\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{3})),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{2}),i_{3})\}
, (so that
\pi_{i_{2}}=i_{2}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{3}}=i_{3}
). For all such terms, equation (
393
) of
Lem.

13
implies
\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}},Y_{j_{3}},Y_{j_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{j_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{4}}})\big]=\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{U}
when
j_{1}\neq j_{4}
(respectively,
j_{2}\neq j_{3}
), and
\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}},Y_{j_{3}},Y_{j_{4}})h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{j_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{4}}})\big]=0
when
j_{1}\neq j_{4}
(respectively,
j_{2}\neq j_{3}
), since
h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}},Y_{j_{3}},Y_{j_{4}})=0
. The number of terms in
(II)
is
4s(s-2)(s-4)n^{4}
.

•

(III)
includes the terms for which all indices
j_{1},\dots,j_{4},\texttt{swap}(j_{1}),\dots,\texttt{swap}(j_{4})
are different and
for which there are exactly two pairs of repeated bin indices among
i_{1},\dots,i_{4},\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{1}),\dots,\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{4})
, and these two pairs are one of the following:
\{(i_{1},i_{2}),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{1}),\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{2}))\}
,
\{(i_{1},\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{2})),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{1}),i_{2})\}
(so that
\pi_{i_{1}}=i_{1}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{2}}=i_{2}
);
\{(i_{1},i_{3}),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{1}),\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{3}))\}
,
\{(i_{1},\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{3})),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{1}),i_{3})\}
(both of which imply that
\pi_{i_{1}}=i_{1}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{2}}=i_{2}
);
\{(i_{2},i_{4}),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{2}),\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{4}))\}
,
\{(i_{2},\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{4})),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{2}),i_{4})\}
(both of which imply that
\pi_{i_{2}}=i_{2}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{4}}=i_{4}
); or
\{(i_{3},i_{4}),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{3}),\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{4}))\}
,
\{(i_{3},\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{4})),(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i_{3}),i_{4})\}
(both of which imply that
\pi_{i_{3}}=i_{3}\Leftrightarrow\pi_{i_{4}}=i_{4}
). By equation (
393
) of
Lem.

13
, for all such terms we can write
\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}},Y_{j_{3}},Y_{j_{4}})\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{j_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{4}}})\big]\big]=\frac{3}{8}\mathcal{U}
. The number of terms in
(III)
is
8s(s-2)(s-4)n^{3}(n-1)\leq 8s(s-2)(s-4)n^{4}
.

•

(IV)
includes the terms for which all indices
j_{1},\dots,j_{4},\texttt{swap}(j_{1}),\dots,\texttt{swap}(j_{4})
are different and which do not belong to either
(I)
,
(II)
or
(III)
.
For such terms, we have that
\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Y}}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}},Y_{j_{3}},Y_{j_{4}})\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{Z}}(Z_{\pi_{j_{1}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{2}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{3}}},Z_{\pi_{j_{4}}})\big]\big]\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}
, by equation (
391
).

•

(V)
includes all terms for which
(j_{1},\dots,j_{4})
belongs to
(\mathsf{J}_{1}\cup\mathsf{J}_{2})^{c}
, where
\mathsf{J}_{1}
and
\mathsf{J}_{2}
are defined in (
E.3.1
). The total contribution of such terms can be upper-bounded by
\frac{4n^{2}-11n+6}{n^{3}}\tilde{\xi}
by the same argument as in the standard permutation case.

We conclude that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[V_{n}^{\pi,s}]

\textstyle\leq\underbrace{\frac{s(s-2)(s-4)(s-6)}{s^{4}}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{U}}_{(I)}+\underbrace{\frac{4s(s-2)(s-4)}{s^{4}}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{U}}_{(II)}+\underbrace{\frac{8s(s-2)(s-4)}{s^{4}}\times\frac{3}{8}\mathcal{U}}_{(III)}

(413)

\textstyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{s^{4}-s(s-2)(s-4)(s-6)-12s(s-2)(s-4)}{s^{4}}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}}_{(IV)}+\underbrace{\frac{10n^{2}-44n+48}{n^{3}}\tilde{\xi}}_{(V)}

(414)

\textstyle=\frac{1}{4}\big(1+\frac{s^{2}+s-4}{s^{3}}\big)\mathcal{U}+\frac{10n^{2}-44n+48}{n^{3}}\tilde{\xi}.

(415)

Appendix F
Proof of

Corollary

3
:
Corollary 3

(Power of cheap independence: finite variance, PD)
.

To establish the standard testing claim, we will show that
70
implies the sufficient power condition
319
in the proof of
Thm.

2
.
Our proof makes use of three lemmas, proved in
Secs.

F.3
,
F.1
, and
F.2
respectively, that relate parameters of
319
to
\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})
.

Lemma 14

(
\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]
bound)
.

Under the assumptions of
Corollary

3
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]\geq 4\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})=\mathcal{U}.

(416)

Lemma 15

(
\psi_{1}^{\prime}
bound)
.

Under the assumptions of
Corollary

3
,

\textstyle\psi_{1}^{\prime}\leq 4\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\xi.

(417)

for
\psi^{\prime}_{1}
as defined in equation
Sec.

4.2
and
\xi
as defined in
Corollary

3
.

Lemma 16

(
\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}
bound)
.

Under the assumptions of
Corollary

3
,

\textstyle\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\leq 4\tilde{\xi}_{\textup{wild}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}),

(418)

for
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}
as defined in equation
Sec.

4.2
and
\xi
as defined in
Corollary

3
.

Since
\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]\geq 4\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})=\mathcal{U}
by
Lem.

14
,
equation
319
holds whenever

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle 3\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})&\textstyle\geq\sqrt{(\frac{3}{\beta}-1)\big(\frac{16\psi_{1}^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{144\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}+\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\frac{32\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{960\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}+\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi}.\end{split}

Next, by
Lems.

15
and
16
, we can upper-bound the right-hand side of this expression by

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\sqrt{(\frac{3}{\beta}-1)\big(\frac{64\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\xi}{n}+\frac{144\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad+\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\frac{128\tilde{\xi}_{\textup{wild}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})+32\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})}{n}+\frac{960\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}+\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\frac{(256\tilde{\xi}_{\textup{wild}}+32\xi)\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})}{n}}+\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\frac{32\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})}{n}}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad+\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\frac{960\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}}+\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi},\end{split}

where we used that
\frac{3}{\beta}-1\leq\frac{1}{4}\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)
.
Hence, for
319
to hold, it suffices to have

\textstyle ax^{2}

\textstyle-bx-c\geq 0,

(419)

\textstyle\text{with }\qquad x

\textstyle=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}),\qquad a=3-\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\frac{32}{n}},

(420)

\textstyle b

\textstyle=\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\frac{(256\tilde{\xi}_{\textup{wild}}+32\xi)}{n}},\qquad c=\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\frac{960\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}}+\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi}.

(421)

Equation
319
therefore holds when
x\geq\frac{b}{a}+\sqrt{\frac{c}{a}}\geq\frac{b+\sqrt{b^{2}+4ac}}{2a}
, concluding the proof of
70
.

To establish the cheap testing claim, we will show that
68
implies the sufficient power condition
321

in the proof of
Thm.

2
.
Since
\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]\geq\mathcal{U}
by
Lem.

14
,

321
holds when

\textstyle\big(3\!-\!\frac{s+1}{s^{2}}\big)\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\!\geq\!\sqrt{(\frac{3}{\beta}-1)\big(\frac{16\psi_{1}^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{144\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}

(422)

\textstyle+\!\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{\prime}\big(\frac{32}{n}+\frac{2304}{ns}+\frac{460800}{ns^{2}}\big)+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big(\frac{960}{n^{2}}+\frac{4147200}{n^{2}s}\big)\big)}\!+\!\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi}.

(423)

Next, by
Lems.

15
and
16
, we can upper-bound the right-hand side of this expression by

\textstyle\sqrt{(\frac{3}{\beta}-1)\big(\frac{64\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\xi}{n}+\frac{144\psi_{2}^{\prime}}{n^{2}}\big)}+\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi}

(424)

\textstyle\quad+\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)^{1/2}\big((4\tilde{\xi}_{\textup{wild}}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{4}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}))(\frac{32}{n}+\frac{2304}{ns}+\frac{460800}{ns^{2}})

(425)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big(\frac{960}{n^{2}}+\frac{4147200}{n^{2}s}\big)\big)^{1/2}

(426)

\textstyle\leq(3-\frac{s+1}{s^{2}}-a)\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})+b\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})+c\quad\text{for}\quad

(427)

\textstyle a

\textstyle\triangleq 3-\frac{s+1}{s^{2}}-\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\frac{32}{n}+\frac{2304}{ns}+\frac{460800}{ns^{2}}\big)},

(428)

\textstyle b

\textstyle\triangleq\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\tilde{\xi}_{\textup{wild}}(\frac{256}{n}+\frac{18432}{ns}+\frac{3686400}{ns^{2}})+\frac{32\xi}{n}\big)},\quad\text{and}\quad

(429)

\textstyle c

\textstyle\triangleq\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\psi_{2}^{\prime}\big(\frac{960}{n^{2}}+\frac{4147200}{n^{2}s}\big)}+\frac{10}{n}\tilde{\xi}.

(430)

The sufficiency of
68
now follows from the sufficiency of the quadratic inequality
ax^{2}-bx-c\geq 0
with
x=\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})
, exactly as in the standard case, and the triangle inequality.

F.1
Proof of

Lem.

14
:
Lemma 14

(
\mathbb{E}[V_{n}]
bound)
.

Let

(\widehat{\Pi}^{\prime},\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime},\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime})
represent an independent copy of
(\widehat{\Pi},\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Then, two applications of Cauchy-Schwarz and
[
51
, App. B]
imply that

\textstyle\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})=\mathbb{E}\big[\iint\mathbf{k}(x,x^{\prime})\,d(\widehat{\Pi}-\widehat{\mathbb{P}}\times\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})(x)\,d(\widehat{\Pi}^{\prime}-\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}\times\widehat{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime})(x^{\prime})\big]

(431)
