(534)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}

(535)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|(\mu_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\big).

(536)

We now turn to bounding
\|\bar{A}\|^{2}_{F}
in terms of
\mathcal{A}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{A}^{(2)}
using Jensen’s inequality:

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle 16s^{4}\sum_{i,j=1}^{s/2}\bar{a}_{ij}^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{s/2}\big(\big(\|(\mu_{j}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(j)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times\big(\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(\|(\mu_{j}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(j)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\big)^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{s/2}\big(2\big(2\|(\mu_{j}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+2\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(j)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times\big(2\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+2\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad+2\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(4\|(\mu_{j}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+4\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+4\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(j)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+4\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq 4\big(\sum_{j=1}^{s}\|(\mu_{j}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)\big(\sum_{i=1}^{s}\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad+4s\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(\sum_{j=1}^{s}\|(\mu_{j}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{s}\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=4(\mathcal{A}^{(1)})^{2}(\mathcal{A}^{(2)})^{2}+4s\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big((\mathcal{A}^{(1)})^{2}+(\mathcal{A}^{(2)})^{2}\big).\end{split}

Next, we bound
\mathrm{Tr}(\bar{A})
in terms of
\mathcal{A}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{A}^{(2)}
using Cauchy-Schwarz:

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle 4s^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{s/2}\bar{a}_{ii}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{s/2}\big(\big(\|(\mu_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times\big(\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(\|(\mu_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq 2\big(\sum_{i=1}^{s}\|(\mu_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)^{1/2}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{s}\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)^{1/2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle+\sqrt{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\|(\mu_{i}\!-\!\Pi\!+\!\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}\!+\!\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}\!-\!\Pi\!+\!\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=2\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\mathcal{A}^{(2)}+\sqrt{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(\mathcal{A}^{(1)}+\mathcal{A}^{(2)}\big).\end{split}

Finally, we bound
\|{\bar{b}}\|_{2}^{2}
in terms of
\mathcal{A}^{(1)}
,
\mathcal{A}^{(2)}
,
\mathcal{A}^{(3)}
and
\mathcal{A}^{(4)}
:

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle 16s^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{s/2}\bar{b}_{i}^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{s/2}\big(\|(\mu-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big(\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\quad+\big(\|(\mu_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\|(\tilde{\mu}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(2\|(\mu-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+2\|(\tilde{\mu}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|(\mu_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}+\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big)\big)^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq 12\sum_{i=1}^{s/2}\big(\|(\mu-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big(\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\quad+\big(\|(\mu_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)\|(\tilde{\mu}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\quad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})^{2}\big(2\|(\mu-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+2\|(\tilde{\mu}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+\|(\tilde{\mu}_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|(\mu_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}+\|(\mu_{\texttt{swap}_{s}(i)}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}\big)\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle=\frac{12(\mathcal{A}^{(2)})^{2}(\mathcal{A}^{(3)})^{2}}{s}+\frac{12(\mathcal{A}^{(1)})^{2}(\mathcal{A}^{(4)})^{2}}{s}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\qquad+12\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big((\mathcal{A}^{(1)})^{2}+(\mathcal{A}^{(2)})^{2}+(\mathcal{A}^{(3)})^{2}+(\mathcal{A}^{(4)})^{2}\big).\end{split}

G.6
Proof of

Lem.

22
:
Lemma 22

(
\mathcal{A}^{(i)}
mean bounds)
.

G.6.1
Bounding
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{A}^{(1)}]
and
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{A}^{(2)}]

By the triangle inequality,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\|(\mu_{i}-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}}\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}^{(i)}},\Pi)^{2}}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}({\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(i)}}}\!\!\times\!{\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i))}}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}}\big]\\
\textstyle&\textstyle+\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}({\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}}\times{\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(i)}}},\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})^{2}}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}({\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}}\!\times\!{\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i))}}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}}\big].\end{split}

Cauchy-Schwarz and the expected squared MMD bound of
457
now yield

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}^{(i)}},\Pi)^{2}}\big]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}^{(i)}},\Pi)^{2}\big]}\leq\sqrt{\frac{sK}{m}}.

(537)

Similarly, the triangle inequality, Cauchy-Schwarz, and the expected squared MMD bounds of
Sec.

G.1
yield

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}({\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}}\!\times\!{\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(i)}}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}}\big]

(538)

\textstyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}({\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}}\!\times\!{\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(i)}}},\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}({\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}}\!\times\!{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}}\big]

(539)

\textstyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}({\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}}\!\times\!{\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(i)}}},\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}\big]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}({\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}}\!\times\!{\mathbb{Q}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}\big]}

(540)

\textstyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{sK}{m}}+\sqrt{\frac{sK}{n}}.

(541)

Parallel reasoning yields the bounds

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}({\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}}\!\times\!{\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i))}}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}}\big]

\textstyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{sK}{m}}+\sqrt{\frac{sK}{n}}\quad\text{and}\quad

(542)

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(i)}}\!\!\times\!\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i))}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}}\big]

\textstyle\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{sK}{m}}.

(543)

Hence
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{A}^{(1)}]\leq D_{1}
as advertised, and
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{A}^{(2)}]\leq D_{2}
follows from parallel reasoning.

G.6.2
Bounding
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{A}^{(3)}]
and
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{A}^{(4)}]

Since
\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(j)}}\!\!\times\!\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(j))}}=\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}
, independence and our prior MMD moment bounds imply that

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\frac{2}{s}\sum_{j=1}^{s/2}\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(j)}}\!\!\times\!\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(j))}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}]=\frac{4}{s^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s/2}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(j)}}\!\!\times\!\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(j))}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}]

(544)

\textstyle\leq\frac{8}{s^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s/2}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(j)}}\!\!\times\!\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}]+\frac{8}{s^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s/2}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(j)}}\!\!\times\!\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})^{2}]\leq\frac{8K}{n}.

(545)

Hence, by the triangle inequality, Cauchy-Schwarz, and our prior MMD moment bounds

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{A}^{(3)}]=\sqrt{s}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\mu-\Pi+\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\mathbf{k}\|_{\mathbf{k}}\big]\leq\sqrt{s}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{X}},\Pi)+2\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{\mathbb{Y}}\times\delta_{\mathbb{Z}},\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})

(546)

\textstyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\frac{2}{s}\sum_{j=1}^{s/2}\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(j)}}\!\!\times\!\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(j))}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\frac{2}{s}\sum_{j=\frac{s}{2}+1}^{s}\delta_{\mathbb{Y}^{(j)}}\!\!\times\!\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(j))}},\mathbb{P}\!\times\!\mathbb{Q})]

(547)

\textstyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{sK}{n}}+4\sqrt{\frac{sK}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{8sK}{n}}.

(548)

Analogous reasoning gives

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{A}^{(4)}]\leq D_{4}
, completing the proof.

G.7
Proof of

Lem.

23
:
Lemma 23

(
\mathcal{A}^{(i)}
complementary quantile bounds)
.

Fix any
k\in[n]
, let
\tilde{X}=(\tilde{Y},\tilde{Z})
be an independent draw from
\Pi
, and let
\tilde{\mathbb{X}}=(X_{1},\dots,X_{k-1},\tilde{X},X_{k},\dots,X_{n})=(\tilde{\mathbb{Y}},\tilde{\mathbb{Z}})
.
Viewing
\mathcal{A}^{(1)}
as a function of
\mathbb{X}
and using the triangle inequality, we can write

\textstyle|\mathcal{A}^{(1)}(\mathbb{X})-\mathcal{A}^{(1)}(\tilde{\mathbb{X}})|\leq\frac{1}{m}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{X_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{X}})+\frac{2}{m}\mathrm{MMD}_{g_{Z}}(\delta_{Z_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Z}})\sqrt{(\delta_{\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}}\times\delta_{\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}})g_{Y}}

(549)

\textstyle+\frac{1}{m}\mathrm{MMD}_{g_{Y}}(\delta_{Y_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Y}})\sqrt{\sup_{z}g_{Z}(z,z)}+\frac{1}{m}\mathrm{MMD}_{g_{Z}}(\delta_{Z_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Z}})\sqrt{\sup_{y}g_{Y}(y,y)}

(550)

\textstyle+\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{MMD}_{g_{Y}}(\delta_{Y_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Y}})\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathrm{MMD}_{g_{Z}}^{2}(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(i)}},\delta_{\mathbb{Z}^{(\texttt{swap}_{s}(i))}})}\leq\frac{10\sqrt{K}}{m}+\frac{2\sqrt{sK}}{n}.

(551)

Viewing
\mathcal{A}^{(3)}
as a function of
\mathbb{X}
and using the triangle inequality, we can also write

\textstyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}|\mathcal{A}^{(3)}(\mathbb{X})-\mathcal{A}^{(3)}(\tilde{\mathbb{X}})|\leq\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\delta_{X_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{X}})+\frac{2}{n}\mathrm{MMD}_{g_{Z}}(\delta_{Z_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Z}})\sqrt{(\delta_{\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}}\times\delta_{\tilde{\mathbb{Y}}})g_{Y}}

(552)

\textstyle+\frac{2}{n}\mathrm{MMD}_{g_{Y}}(\delta_{Y_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Y}})\sqrt{(\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}\times\delta_{\mathbb{Z}})g_{Y}}+\frac{1}{sm}\mathrm{MMD}_{g_{Y}}(\delta_{Y_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Y}})\sqrt{\sup_{z}g_{Z}(z,z)}

(553)

\textstyle+\frac{1}{sm}\mathrm{MMD}_{g_{Z}}(\delta_{Z_{k}},\delta_{\tilde{Z}})\sqrt{\sup_{y}g_{Y}(y,y)}\leq\frac{14\sqrt{K}}{n}.

(554)

Parallel reasoning additionally yields the bounds

\textstyle|\mathcal{A}^{(2)}(\mathbb{X})-\mathcal{A}^{(2)}(\tilde{\mathbb{X}})|\leq\frac{6\sqrt{K}}{m}\quad\text{and}\quad|\mathcal{A}^{(4)}(\mathbb{X})-\mathcal{A}^{(4)}(\tilde{\mathbb{X}})|\leq\frac{6\sqrt{Ks}}{n}.

(555)

The advertised result now follows from the bounded differences inequality (
Lem.

18
) and the mean estimates of
Lem.

22
.

G.8
Proof of

Lem.

24
:
Lemma 24

(Quantile bound for independence permutation threshold)
.

G.8.1
Bounding
\tilde{V}^{\pi,s}
in terms of
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)

Fix any
\alpha,\beta\in(0,1)
.
By
Lem.

21
,

\textstyle\tilde{V}_{n}^{\pi,s}

\textstyle=\epsilon^{\top}A\epsilon+b^{\top}\epsilon+c

(556)

\textstyle=\epsilon^{\top}\bar{A}\epsilon+\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})}{s^{2}}\epsilon^{\top}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\epsilon+\bar{b}^{\top}\epsilon+\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})}{s}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\epsilon+\bar{c}+\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})}{4}

(557)

\textstyle\leq|\epsilon^{\top}\bar{A}\epsilon-\mathrm{Tr}(\bar{A})|+\mathrm{Tr}(\bar{A})+\bar{b}^{\top}\epsilon+\big|\bar{c}\big|+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\epsilon^{\top}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\epsilon}{s^{2}}+\frac{\mathbf{1}^{\top}\epsilon}{s}\big).

(558)

where
\epsilon\sim\textnormal{Unif}(\{\pm 1\}^{s/2})
is independent of
\mathbb{X}
,
A
,
b
, and
c
were defined in
520
,
and
\bar{A}
,
\bar{b}
, and
\bar{c}
were defined in
Sec.

G.5
.

Now fix any
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,0.86)
and
\delta^{\prime\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
.
By equation
208
of the Hanson-Wright inequality (
Lem.

6
), with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}
,

\textstyle|\epsilon^{\top}\bar{A}\epsilon-\mathrm{Tr}(\bar{A})|=|\epsilon^{\top}\bar{A}\epsilon-\mathbb{E}[\epsilon^{\top}\bar{A}\epsilon]|\leq\frac{20\|\bar{A}\|_{\text{F}}\log(1/\delta^{\prime})}{3}.

(559)

Meanwhile, Hoeffding’s inequality
[
29
, Thm. 2]
implies that

\textstyle\bar{b}^{\top}\epsilon\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\bar{b}}}}\right\|_{2}\sqrt{2\log(1/\delta^{\prime\prime})}

\textstyle\quad\text{with probability at least}\quad 1-\delta^{\prime\prime}\quad\text{and}\quad

(560)

\textstyle|\mathbf{1}^{\top}\epsilon|\leq\sqrt{s\log(2/\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})}

\textstyle\quad\text{with probability at least}\quad 1-\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}.

(561)

Hence, with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}
,

\textstyle\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\epsilon^{\top}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\epsilon}{s^{2}}+\frac{\mathbf{1}^{\top}\epsilon}{s}\leq\frac{1}{4}+\frac{s\log(2/\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})}{s^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})}{s}}=\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\log(2/\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})}{s}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})}{s}}.

(562)

Moreover,
Lem.

21
guarantees that

\textstyle|\bar{c}|\leq\frac{1}{4}\big(\frac{(\mathcal{A}^{(3)})^{2}}{s}+\frac{2\mathcal{A}^{(3)}\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})}{\sqrt{s}}\big)

(563)

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle&\textstyle\|\bar{A}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{(\mathcal{A}^{(1)})^{2}(\mathcal{A}^{(2)})^{2}}{4s^{4}}+\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})((\mathcal{A}^{(1)})^{2}+(\mathcal{A}^{(2)})^{2})}{4s^{3}},\\
\textstyle&\textstyle\mathrm{Tr}(\bar{A})\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\mathcal{A}^{(2)}}{2s^{2}}+\frac{\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})(\mathcal{A}^{(1)}+\mathcal{A}^{(2)})}{4s^{3/2}},\quad\text{and}\quad\end{split}

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\bar{b}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{3(\mathcal{A}^{(2)})^{2}(\mathcal{A}^{(3)})^{2}+(\mathcal{A}^{(1)})^{2}(\mathcal{A}^{(4)})^{2}}{4s^{3}}+\frac{3\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})((\mathcal{A}^{(1)})^{2}+(\mathcal{A}^{(2)})^{2}+(\mathcal{A}^{(3)})^{2}+(\mathcal{A}^{(4)})^{2})}{4s^{2}}.

(564)

Hence, if we choose
\delta^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime\prime}=\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}=\alpha/3
, then, by the union bound and the triangle inequality, we have, with probability at least
1-\alpha
,

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\tilde{V}_{n}^{\pi,s}&\textstyle\leq\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)\triangleq\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\mathcal{A}^{(2)}}{s^{2}}\big(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{10\log(3/\alpha)}{3}\big)+\frac{\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}\log(3/\alpha)}(\mathcal{A}^{(2)}\mathcal{A}^{(3)}+\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\mathcal{A}^{(4)})}{s^{3/2}}+\frac{(\mathcal{A}^{(3)})^{2}}{4s}\\
\textstyle&\textstyle+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(1)}+\mathcal{A}^{(2)}}{s^{3/2}}\big(\frac{1}{4}\!+\!\frac{10\log(3/\alpha)}{3}\big)\!+\!\frac{\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}\log(3/\alpha)}(\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\!+\!\mathcal{A}^{(2)}+\mathcal{A}^{(3)}\!+\!\mathcal{A}^{(4)})}{s}+\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(3)}}{2\sqrt{s}}\big)\\
\textstyle&\textstyle+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\log(6/\alpha)}{s}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(6/\alpha)}{s}}\big).\end{split}

G.8.2
Bounding
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)
in terms of
\tilde{\Psi}_{n,s}(\alpha,\beta)

Recall the definitions of
(D_{i})_{i=1}^{4}
and
(C_{i})_{i=1}^{4}
in
Lems.

22
and
23
respectively.
We have

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\frac{C_{1}}{s}&\textstyle=\frac{10\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{sm}}+\frac{2\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{sn}}\leq\frac{12\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}},\ \ \frac{C_{2}}{s}=\frac{6\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{sm}}=\frac{6\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}},\ \ \frac{C_{3}}{\sqrt{s}}=\frac{14\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}},\ \ \text{and}\ \ \frac{C_{4}}{\sqrt{s}}=\frac{6\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}},\end{split}

\textstyle\begin{split}\textstyle\frac{D_{1}}{s}&\textstyle=\frac{5\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{ns}}+\frac{2\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{sm}}\leq\frac{7\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}},\qquad\frac{D_{2}}{s}=\frac{3\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}},\\
\textstyle\frac{D_{3}}{\sqrt{s}}&\textstyle=\frac{(5+\sqrt{8})\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\frac{8\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{D_{4}}{\sqrt{s}}=\frac{(1+\sqrt{8})\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\frac{4\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}.\end{split}

By
Lem.

23
, with probability at least
1-\beta
, the following
\mathcal{A}^{(i)}
-based bounds all hold simultaneously.
First,

\textstyle\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\mathcal{A}^{(2)}}{s^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{s^{2}}\big(D_{1}+C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)\big(D_{2}+C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)

(565)

\textstyle\leq\big(\frac{7\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{12\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{2n}}\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}\big)\times\big(\frac{3\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{6\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{2n}}\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}\big)

(566)

\textstyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\big(21+56\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}+36\log(4/\beta)\big).

(567)

Second,

\textstyle\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(2)}\mathcal{A}^{(3)}+\mathcal{A}^{(1)}\mathcal{A}^{(4)}}{s^{3/2}}

\textstyle\leq\frac{\big(D_{2}+C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)\big(D_{3}+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)+\big(D_{1}+C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)\big(D_{4}+C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)}{s^{3/2}}

(568)

\textstyle\leq\big(\frac{3\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{6\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)\times\big(\frac{14\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{8\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)

(569)

\textstyle\qquad+\big(\frac{7\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{12\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)\times\big(\frac{6\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{4\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)

(570)

\textstyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\big(84+148\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}+48\log(4/\beta)\big).

(571)

Third,

\textstyle\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(3)}}{\sqrt{s}}

\textstyle\leq\frac{D_{3}}{\sqrt{s}}+\frac{C_{3}}{\sqrt{s}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\big(14+8\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)\quad\text{and hence}\quad

(572)

\textstyle\frac{(\mathcal{A}^{(3)})^{2}}{s}

\textstyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\big(196+112\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}+64\log(4/\beta)\big).

(573)

Fourth,

\textstyle\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(1)}+\mathcal{A}^{(2)}}{s^{3/2}}

\textstyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}\big(\frac{D_{1}}{s}+\frac{C_{1}}{s}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}+\frac{D_{2}}{s}+\frac{C_{2}}{s}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)\leq\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{ns}}\big(10+13\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}\big).

(574)

Fifth,

\textstyle\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(1)}+\mathcal{A}^{(2)}+\mathcal{A}^{(3)}+\mathcal{A}^{(4)}}{s}

(575)

\textstyle\leq\frac{D_{1}}{s}+\frac{C_{1}}{s}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}+\frac{D_{2}}{s}+\frac{C_{2}}{s}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}\big(\frac{D_{3}}{\sqrt{s}}+\frac{C_{s}}{\sqrt{s}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}+\frac{D_{4}}{\sqrt{s}}+\frac{C_{4}}{\sqrt{s}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)

(576)

\textstyle=\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\big(30+22\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}\big).

(577)

Hence, with probability at least
1-\beta
, the quantity
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)

Sec.

G.8.1
satisfies

\textstyle\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbb{X},s}(\alpha)

\textstyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\big(21+56\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}+36\log(4/\beta)\big)\times\big(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{10\log(3/\alpha)}{3}\big)

(578)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{K}{n}\big(84+148\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}+48\log(4/\beta)\big)\times\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}\log(3/\alpha)}

(579)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{K}{n}\big(196+112\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}+64\log(4/\beta)\big)

(580)

\textstyle\quad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\Big(\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{ns}}\big(10+13\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}\big)\times\big(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{10\log(3/\alpha)}{3}\big)

(581)

\textstyle\qquad+\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\big(30+22\sqrt{\log(4/\beta)}\big)\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}\log(3/\alpha)}+\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n}}\big(14+8\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\beta)}{2}}\big)\Big)

(582)

\textstyle\quad+\mathrm{MMD}_{\mathbf{k}}^{2}(\Pi,\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q})\big(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\log(6/\alpha)}{s}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(6/\alpha)}{s}}\big)\leq\tilde{\Psi}_{n,s}(\alpha,\beta).

(583)

Appendix H
Proof of

Proposition

2
:
Proposition 2

(Quantile comparison method)
.

Let
{(T_{(b)})}_{b=1}^{B}
represent the auxiliary values
{(T_{b})}_{b=1}^{B}

ordered increasingly.
We begin by relating the random critical value

T_{(b_{\alpha})}
to the conditional quantiles of
T_{1}
given
\mathbb{X}
.

Lemma 25

(High probability bound on critical value
[
16
, Lem. 6, Cor. 1]
)
.

Under the assumptions of
Proposition

2
, let
F_{\mathbb{X}}
be the cumulative distribution function of
T_{1}
given
\mathbb{X}
and let

\textstyle q_{1-\alpha(\delta)}(\mathbb{X})\triangleq\inf\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}:1-\alpha(\delta)\leq F_{\mathbb{X}}(x)\big\}\ \ \text{for}\ \ \alpha(\delta)\triangleq(\frac{\delta}{{B\choose\lfloor{\alpha(B+1)}\rfloor}})^{\frac{1}{\lfloor{\alpha(B+1)}\rfloor}}\geq\frac{\alpha}{2e}\delta^{\frac{1}{\lfloor{\alpha(B+1)}\rfloor}}

(584)

be the
1-\alpha(\delta)
quantile of
T_{1}
given
\mathbb{X}
.
Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\textstyle T_{(b_{\alpha})}\leq q_{1-\alpha(\delta)}(\mathbb{X}).

(585)

Proof.

Lem. 6 and Cor. 1 of
[
16
]
establish this result for the special case of permuted MMD coreset test statistics, but the proof for the general case is identical.
∎

Since
\Psi_{\mathbb{X}}(\alpha)\geq q_{1-\alpha(\beta/3)}(\mathbb{X})
almost surely by assumption
43
,
Lem.

25
implies that

\textstyle\Pr(\mathcal{A}_{1}^{c})\leq\beta/3\quad\text{for}\quad\mathcal{A}_{1}\triangleq\{T_{(b_{\alpha})}\leq\Psi_{\mathbb{X}}(\alpha^{\star})\}.

(586)

Moreover, our assumption
44
on
\Psi_{n}
ensures that

\textstyle\Pr(\mathcal{A}_{2}^{c})\leq\beta/3\quad\text{for}\quad\mathcal{A}_{2}\triangleq\{\Psi_{\mathbb{X}}(\alpha^{\star})\leq\Psi_{n}(\alpha^{\star},\beta/3)\}.

(587)

Since
\Delta(\mathbb{X})
rejects when
T(\mathbb{X})>T_{(b_{\alpha})}
, the acceptance probability is upper-bounded by

\textstyle\Pr(T(\mathbb{X})\leq T_{(b_{\alpha})})

\textstyle=\Pr(T(\mathbb{X})\leq T_{(b_{\alpha})},\ \mathcal{A}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}_{2})+\Pr(T(\mathbb{X})\leq T_{(b_{\alpha})},\ \mathcal{A}_{1}^{c}\cup\mathcal{A}_{2}^{c})

(588)

\textstyle\leq\Pr(T(\mathbb{X})\leq\Psi_{n}(\alpha^{\star},\beta/3))+\Pr(\mathcal{A}_{1}^{c}\cup\mathcal{A}_{2}^{c})

(589)

\textstyle\leq\Pr(T(\mathbb{X})\leq\mathcal{T}-\Phi_{n}(\frac{\beta}{3}))+\Pr(\mathcal{A}_{1}^{c})+\Pr(\mathcal{A}_{2}^{c})\leq\beta,

(590)

where the final two inequalities used the union bound and our assumptions
41
and
42
.

Appendix I
Proof of

Corollary

1
:
Corollary 1

(Refined two moments method)
.

Our choices of
\Phi_{n}
and
\Psi_{\mathbb{X}}
satisfy the requirements
42
and
43
by Cantelli’s inequality
[
8
, (18)]
.
Moreover, Markov’s inequality
[
42
]
and Cantelli’s inequality imply

\textstyle\Pr(\sqrt{(\alpha^{-1}-1)\mathrm{Var}(T_{1}|\mathbb{X})}\geq\sqrt{(\alpha^{-1}-1)\beta^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(T_{1}|\mathbb{X})]}\mid\mathbb{X})

(591)

\textstyle\quad=\Pr((\alpha^{-1}-1)\mathrm{Var}(T_{1}|\mathbb{X})\geq(\alpha^{-1}-1)\beta^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}(T_{1}|\mathbb{X})]\mid\mathbb{X})\leq\beta\quad\text{and}\quad

(592)

\textstyle\Pr(\mathbb{E}[T_{1}|\mathbb{X}]-\mathbb{E}[T_{1}]\geq\sqrt{(2\beta^{-1}-1)\mathrm{Var}(\mathbb{E}[T_{1}|\mathbb{X}])})\leq\beta/2

(593)

for all
\beta\in(0,1]
.
Hence, our
\Psi_{n}
choice
46
satisfies
44
when
\mathbb{E}[T_{1}\mid\mathbb{X}]=0
almost surely and, by the union bound, our
\Psi_{n}
choice
47
satisfies
44
more generally.

Appendix J
Proofs of minimax optimality results

J.1
Proof of

Proposition

3
:
Proposition 3

(Power of cheap testing: discrete
L^{2}
homogeneity)
.

Introduce the shorthand
t\triangleq s\sqrt[3]{\rho_{n_{1}n_{2}}}
,
u\triangleq\frac{\beta\alpha^{\star}}{24(1-\alpha^{\star})}
, and
v\triangleq\sqrt{t^{3}u}-1
.

Thm.

1
and
the facts that
\psi_{Y,1},\psi_{Z,1}\leq 4\sqrt{b_{(1)}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}
and
\psi_{YZ,2}\leq b_{(1)}
by
[
31
, App. G]
imply that the cheap discrete homogeneity permutation test has power at least
1-\beta
whenever

\textstyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\!=\!\mathbb{E}[U_{n_{1},n_{2}}]

\textstyle\geq\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{16(3-\beta)\sqrt{b_{(1)}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\beta}\big(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\!+\!\frac{1}{n_{2}}\big)}+\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha^{\star}}{\alpha^{\star}}\frac{(36n_{1}^{2}+36n_{2}^{2}+198n_{1}n_{2})b_{(1)}}{\beta n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}}\bigg)

(594)

\textstyle\qquad\times\!(1\!+\!\frac{1}{v})+\frac{\sqrt{\frac{201s^{2}}{4n^{2}}b_{(1)}+{\frac{48s}{n}\sqrt{b_{(1)}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{}}}{v}.

(595)

By the triangle inequality, it suffices to have
ax^{2}-bx-c\geq 0
for
x=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}
,
a=1
,

\textstyle b

\textstyle=\sqrt{\frac{16(3-\beta)\sqrt{b_{(1)}}}{\beta}\big(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\!+\!\frac{1}{n_{2}}\big)}\!\times\!(1\!+\!\frac{1}{v})+\sqrt{\frac{48\sqrt{b_{(1)}}}{n\sqrt[3]{\rho_{n_{1}n_{2}}}}}\,\frac{\sqrt{t}}{v},\quad\text{and}\quad

(596)

\textstyle c

\textstyle=\sqrt{\frac{1-\alpha^{\star}}{\alpha^{\star}}\frac{(36n_{1}^{2}+36n_{2}^{2}+198n_{1}n_{2})b_{(1)}}{\beta n_{1}(n_{1}-1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}}\!\times\!(1+\frac{1}{v})+\frac{\sqrt{201b_{(1)}}}{2n\sqrt[3]{\rho_{n_{1}n_{2}}}}\,\frac{t}{v}.

(597)

Hence, by the quadratic formula, it suffices to have
x\geq b+\sqrt{c}\geq(b+\sqrt{b^{2}+4ac})/(2a)
.
Finally, since
1/(n\sqrt[3]{\rho_{n_{1}n_{2}}})=O(1/n_{1})
, there exist universal constants
C,C^{\prime}
such that

\textstyle b+\sqrt{c}

\textstyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}b_{(1)}^{1/4}}{\sqrt{\min(\beta,\alpha^{\star})\,n_{1}}}(1+\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\min(v,\sqrt{v})})=\frac{C^{\prime}b_{(1)}^{1/4}}{\sqrt{\min(\beta,\alpha^{\star})\,n_{1}}}(1+\frac{(1+v)^{1/3}}{u^{1/6}\min(v,\sqrt{v})})

(598)

\textstyle\leq\frac{Cb_{(1)}^{1/4}}{\sqrt[6]{\beta\alpha^{\star}}\sqrt{\min(\beta,\alpha^{\star})\,n_{1}}}(1+\frac{1}{v}).

(599)

J.2
Proof of

Proposition

4
:
Proposition 4

(Power of cheap testing: Hölder
L^{2}
homogeneity)
.

Let
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
be a discrete distribution over
\{B_{1},\dots,B_{K}\}
with probability
\int_{B_{k}}d\mathbb{P}(x)
of selecting bin
B_{k}
for each
k\in[K]
. Define
\tilde{\mathbb{Q}}
analogously based on
\mathbb{Q}
, and let
\kappa_{(1)}=\lfloor n_{1}^{2/(4\tau+d)}\rfloor
.
By
[
2
, Lem. 3]
,

\textstyle\|{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}-\tilde{\mathbb{Q}}}\|_{2}^{2}\geq C_{1}\kappa_{(1)}^{-d}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2},

(600)

for a universal constant
C_{1}
.
Furthermore, by the argument in
[
31
, App. I]
,

\textstyle b_{(1)}\triangleq\max\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\}\leq L\kappa_{(1)}^{-d}.

(601)

These bounds, together with
Proposition

3
, yield the sufficient power condition

\textstyle\frac{C^{\prime}L^{1/4}\kappa_{(1)}^{-d/4}}{\sqrt[6]{\beta\alpha^{\star}}\sqrt{\min(\beta,\alpha^{\star})\,n_{1}}}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{s^{3}\frac{\beta\alpha^{\star}}{24(1-\alpha^{\star})}\rho_{n_{1}n_{2}}}-1}\bigg)\leq\sqrt{C_{1}}\kappa_{(1)}^{-d/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\|{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}-\tilde{\mathbb{Q}}}\|_{2}

(602)

for a universal constant
C^{\prime}
.
The result now follows from the upper estimate
n_{1}^{2/(4\tau+d)}\geq\kappa_{(1)}
.

J.3
Proof of

Proposition

5
:
Proposition 5

(Power of cheap testing: discrete
L^{2}
independence)
.

Note that the augmentation variables
(A_{i})_{i=1}^{n},(B_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\overset{\textup{\tiny{i.i.d.}\ }}{\sim}\textnormal{Unif}((0,1))
are, with probability
1
, all distinct.
Therefore,
g_{Y}((Y_{i},A_{i}),(Y_{i},A_{i}))=g_{Z}((Z_{i},B_{i}),(Z_{i},B_{i}))=0
for all
i\in[n]
.
The arguments of

[
31
, Prop. 5.3]
imply the variance component
Sec.

4.2
bounds

\textstyle\psi^{\prime}_{1}

\textstyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{b_{(2)}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2},\quad\psi^{\prime}_{2}\leq C_{2}b_{(2)},\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{1}\leq C_{3}\sqrt{b_{(2)}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(603)

for universal constants
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
, and
C_{3}
. Additionally, we can bound the mean component
\tilde{\xi}

37
by bounding the following expression for arbitrary
i_{1},\dots,i_{4},i^{\prime}_{1},\dots,i^{\prime}_{4}\in[8]
.

\textstyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[h_{\mathrm{in}}\big(((Y_{i_{1}},A_{i_{1}}),(Z_{i^{\prime}_{1}},B_{i^{\prime}_{1}})),((Y_{i_{2}},A_{i_{2}}),(Z_{i^{\prime}_{2}},B_{i^{\prime}_{2}})),

(604)

\textstyle\qquad\quad((Y_{i_{3}},A_{i_{3}}),(Z_{i^{\prime}_{3}},B_{i^{\prime}_{3}})),((Y_{i_{4}},A_{i_{4}}),(Z_{i^{\prime}_{4}},B_{i^{\prime}_{4}})\big)\big]\big|

(605)

\textstyle=\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\big(g_{Y}((Y_{i_{1}},A_{i_{1}}),(Y_{i_{2}},A_{i_{2}}))+g_{Y}((Y_{i_{3}},A_{i_{3}}),(Y_{i_{4}},A_{i_{4}}))

(606)

\textstyle\qquad-g_{Y}((Y_{i_{1}},A_{i_{1}}),(Y_{i_{3}},A_{i_{3}}))-g_{Y}((Y_{i_{2}},A_{i_{2}}),(Y_{i_{4}},A_{i_{4}}))\big)

(607)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\times\big(g_{Z}((Z_{i^{\prime}_{1}},B_{i^{\prime}_{1}}),(Z_{i^{\prime}_{2}},B_{i^{\prime}_{2}}))+g_{Z}((Z_{i^{\prime}_{3}},B_{i^{\prime}_{3}}),(Z_{i^{\prime}_{4}},B_{i^{\prime}_{4}}))

(608)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\quad-g_{Z}((Z_{i^{\prime}_{1}},B_{i^{\prime}_{1}}),(Z_{i^{\prime}_{3}},B_{i^{\prime}_{3}}))-g_{Z}((Z_{i^{\prime}_{2}},B_{i^{\prime}_{2}}),(Z_{i^{\prime}_{4}},B_{i^{\prime}_{4}}))\big)\big]\big|

(609)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{i_{1}}=Y_{i_{2}},i_{1}\neq i_{2}}+\mathbf{1}_{Y_{i_{3}}=Y_{i_{4}},i_{3}\neq i_{4}}

(610)

\textstyle\qquad-\mathbf{1}_{Y_{i_{1}}=Y_{i_{3}},i_{1}\neq i_{3}}-\mathbf{1}_{Y_{i_{2}}=Y_{i_{4}},i_{2}\neq i_{4}}\big)

(611)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\times\big(\mathbf{1}_{Z_{i^{\prime}_{1}}=Z_{i^{\prime}_{2}},i^{\prime}_{1}\neq i^{\prime}_{2}}+\mathbf{1}_{Z_{i^{\prime}_{3}}=Z_{i^{\prime}_{4}},i^{\prime}_{3}\neq i^{\prime}_{4}}

(612)

\textstyle\qquad\qquad\quad-\mathbf{1}_{Z_{i^{\prime}_{1}}=Z_{i^{\prime}_{3}},i^{\prime}_{1}\neq i^{\prime}_{3}}-\mathbf{1}_{Z_{i^{\prime}_{2}}=Z_{i^{\prime}_{2}},i^{\prime}_{3}\neq i^{\prime}_{4}}\big)\big]

(613)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}16\max\big\{\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}_{Y_{1}=Y_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{Z_{i}=Z_{j}}]:(i,j)\in\{(1,2),(1,3),(3,4)\}\big\}

(614)

\textstyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{=}}16\max\big\{\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}_{Y_{1}=Y_{2},A_{1}\neq A_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{Z_{i}=Z_{j},B_{i}\neq B_{j}}]:(i,j)\in\{(1,2),(1,3),(3,4)\}\big\}

(615)

\textstyle=16\max\big\{\mathbb{E}[g_{Y}((Y_{1},A_{1}),(Y_{2},A_{2}))^{2}g_{Z}((Z_{i},B_{i}),(Z_{j},B_{j}))^{2}]:(i,j)\in\{(1,2),(1,3),(3,4)\}\big\}

(616)

\textstyle=\psi^{\prime}_{2}.

(617)

Above, equality (i) holds because, with probability 1,
A_{i}=A_{j}\Leftrightarrow i=j
and
B_{i}=B_{j}\Leftrightarrow i=j
. Inequality (ii) holds because, after expanding the product, the only terms that remain are of the form
\mathbf{1}_{Y_{i}=Y_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{Z_{k}=Z_{l}}
with
i\neq j
and
k\neq l
. Equality (iii) holds because
A_{1}\neq A_{2}
and
B_{i}\neq B_{j}
almost surely. We conclude that

\textstyle\tilde{\xi}\leq\psi^{\prime}_{2}\leq C_{2}b_{(2)}.

(618)

Since
\mathcal{U}=4\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}
,
Thm.

2
, our mean and variance component bounds, and the triangle inequality imply that the cheap discrete independence test has power at least
1-\beta
whenever
ax^{2}-bx-c\geq 0
for
x=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}
,
a=1-\frac{s+1}{3s^{2}}
,

\textstyle\frac{3}{4}b

\textstyle=\sqrt{(\frac{3}{\beta}\!-\!1)\frac{16C_{1}\sqrt{b_{(2)}}}{n}}\!+\!\sqrt{C_{3}\sqrt{b_{(2)}}}\big(\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}\!-\!2\big)\frac{32}{n}}+\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\big(\frac{2304}{ns}+\frac{460800}{ns^{2}}\big)}\big),\text{ and}

(619)

\textstyle\frac{3}{4}c

\textstyle=\sqrt{C_{2}b_{(2)}}\big(\sqrt{(\frac{3}{\beta}\!-\!1)\big(\frac{144}{n^{2}}\big)}\!+\!\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}\!-\!2\big)\big(\!\frac{960}{n^{2}}\big)}+\frac{24}{n}+\frac{16}{n}+\sqrt{\big(\frac{12}{\alpha^{\star}\beta}-2\big)\frac{4147200}{n^{2}s}}\big).

(620)

Since
a\leq 1
, it therefore suffices to have
x\geq\frac{1}{a}(b+\sqrt{c})\geq\frac{b}{a}+\sqrt{\frac{c}{a}}\geq\frac{b+\sqrt{b^{2}+4ac}}{2a}

by the quadratic formula. The claim now follows as

\frac{1}{a}(b+\sqrt{c})\leq\frac{C\,b_{(2)}^{1/4}}{\sqrt{\beta\alpha^{\star}n}}
for a universal constant
C
.

J.4
Proof of

Proposition

6
:
Proposition 6

(Power of cheap testing: Hölder
L^{2}
independence)
.

Let
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
be a discrete distribution over
\{B_{1,1},\dots,B_{1,K_{1}}\}
with probability
\int_{B_{1,k}}d\mathbb{P}(y)
of selecting bin
B_{1,k}
for each
k\in[K_{1}]
. Define
\tilde{\mathbb{Q}}
analogously as a discrete distribution over
\{B_{2,1},\dots,B_{2,K_{2}}\}
based on
\mathbb{Q}
and
\tilde{\Pi}

analogously as a discrete distribution over
\{(B_{1,k_{1}},B_{2,k_{2}}):k_{1}\in[K_{1}],k_{2}\in[K_{2}]\}
based on
\Pi
.
Let
\kappa_{(2)}=\lfloor n^{2/(4\tau+d_{1}+d_{2})}\rfloor
.
By
[
2
, Lem. 3]
,

\textstyle\|{\tilde{\Pi}-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\times\tilde{\mathbb{Q}}}\|_{2}^{2}\geq C_{1}\kappa_{(2)}^{-(d_{1}+d_{2})}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2},

(621)

for a universal constant
C_{1}
.
Furthermore, by the argument in
[
31
, App. M]
,

\textstyle b_{(2)}\triangleq\max\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\Pi}}}\right\|_{2}^{2},\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\}\leq L\kappa_{(2)}^{-(d_{1}+d_{2})},

(622)

These bounds, together with
Proposition

5
, yield the sufficient power condition

\textstyle\frac{C^{\prime}L^{1/4}\kappa_{(2)}^{-(d_{1}+d_{2})/4}}{\sqrt{\beta\alpha^{\star}n}}\leq\sqrt{C_{1}}\kappa_{(2)}^{-(d_{1}+d_{2})/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\Pi-\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{Q}}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\|{\tilde{\Pi}-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\times\tilde{\mathbb{Q}}}\|_{2}

(623)

for a universal constant
C^{\prime}
.
The result now follows from the estimate
n^{2/(4\tau+d_{1}+d_{2})}\geq\kappa_{(2)}
.

Appendix K
Supplementary experiment details

All independence experiments were run with Python 3.13.0 on a single AMD EPYC 7V13 CPU, with operating system Ubuntu 22.04.5 LTS.
All Higgs boson testing experiments were run with Python 3.11.10 on a single AMD EPYC 7V13 CPU, with operating system Ubuntu 22.04.5 LTS.
All other homogeneity experiments were run with Python 3.11.10 on a single Intel Xeon Platinum 8268 CPU, with operating system Red Hat Enterprise Linux 9.2 (Plow).

K.1
Selection of the number of permutations
B

To select our common permutation count of
B=1279
for the homogeneity testing experiments, we evaluated the power of the standard MMD test with total sample size
n=16384
and varying permutation count of the form
B=20\times 2^{a}-1
for
a\geq 0
in the
MMD setting
of
Sec.

6.1
.
We followed the same procedure to select the permutation count for independence testing experiments, only using the
HSIC setting
of
Sec.

6.2
with
n=2048
in place of the MMD setting.
In both cases, we found that power saturated after
B=1279
as shown in
Fig.

K.1
.

Figure K.1:
Power and runtime of standard MMD permutation tests of homogeneity (left) and standard HSIC wild bootstrap tests of independence (right) as number of permutations
B
varies.

K.2
Cheap permutation tests for feature-based kernels

When a homogeneity U-statistic (
Def.

2
) has a rank-
r
base function of the form

\textstyle g(y,z)=\sum_{j=1}^{r}f_{j}(y)f_{j}(z),

(624)

each test statistic can be computed in time
\Theta(c_{f}nr)
where
c_{f}
is the maximum time required to evaluate a single feature function
f_{j}
, and cheap permutation can be carried out in
\Theta(Bsr)
additional time with
\Theta(sr)
memory, as described in
Alg.

3
.

Algorithm 3

Cheap
homogeneity testing with feature kernels

Input:

Samples
(Y_{i})_{i=1}^{n_{1}}
,
(Z_{i})_{i=1}^{n_{2}}
, bin count
s
, feature maps
f=(f_{j})_{j=1}^{r}

624
, level
\alpha
, permutation count
B

1

Define
(X_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\triangleq(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n_{1}},Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n_{2}})
for
n\leftarrow n_{1}+n_{2}
,
m\leftarrow n/s
, and
s_{1}\leftarrow{s\,n_{1}/}{n}

2

// Compute sufficient statistics using
\Theta(c_{f}nr)
time and
\Theta(sr)
memory

3

for

i=1,2,\ldots,s

do

F_{i}\leftarrow\sum_{k=1}^{m}f(X_{(i-1)m+k})

;

4

// Compute original and permuted test statistics using
\Theta(Bsr)
elementary operations

5

for

b=0,1,2,\ldots,B

do

6

\pi\leftarrow
identity permutation if
b=0
else
random uniform permutation of
[s]

7

T_{b}\leftarrow\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{s}(2\,\mathbf{1}(\pi_{b}(i)\leq s_{1})-1)F_{i}}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

8

end for

9

// Return rejection probability

10

R\leftarrow 1+
number of permuted statistics
(T_{b})_{b=1}^{B}

smaller than
T_{0}
if ties are broken at random

11

return

\Delta(\mathbb{X})\triangleq\min(1,\max(0,R\!-\!(1\!-\!\alpha)(B\!+\!1)))
